\displaystyle\in{L}^{2}([0,T]\times Y,\Leb\otimes\nu,U^{\prime})\quad

is continuous.

Assumption F. 5
.

There exist a number
\gamma>0
and a constant
{C}_{\gamma}>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375ptH}}}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq{C}_{\gamma}(1+\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.47806pt\hskip 9.47806pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.47806pt\hskip 9.47806ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81377pt\hskip 8.81377pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81377pt\hskip 8.81377ptH}}}),\;u\in H,\;t\in[0,T],

(3.6)

Assumption A. 1
.

{u}_{0}\in{H}
,

f\in{L}^{4+\gamma}([0,T];V^{\prime})
,
where
\gamma
is the number from Assumption F
5
.

Remark 3.2
.

If
Y
is a separable Banach space and
\nu
is a Lévy measure on
Y
, then
\nu(\{0\})=0
, and therefore

\int_{Y}\mathds{1}_{\{0\}}(F(t,x,y))\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)=0\mbox{ for all }x\in H\mbox{ and }t\in[0,T].

Hence, in this case, the corresponding part of Assumption F.
2
in
[
47
]
is satisfied.

Let us observe that by the Hölder inequality, conditions (
3.5
) and (
3.6
) imply that
for every
p\in[2,8+2\gamma]
there exists a constant
{C}_{p}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptp}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptp}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143ptp}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375ptp}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375ptH}}}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq{C}_{p}(1+\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptp}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptp}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.47806pt\hskip 9.47806ptp}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.47806pt\hskip 9.47806ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81377pt\hskip 8.81377ptp}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81377pt\hskip 8.81377ptH}}}),\;u\in H,\;t\in[0,T].

(3.7)

To see this, note that for any
p\in(2,8+2\gamma)
there exist
C_{p}>0
and
\theta\in(0,1)
such that
1/p=(1-\theta)/2+\theta/(8+2\gamma)
and

\displaystyle\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptp}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptp}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143ptp}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375ptp}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375ptH}}}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\left(\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375ptH}}}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\right)^{\frac{p(1-\theta)}{2}}

(3.8)

\displaystyle\left(\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375ptH}}}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\right)^{\frac{p\theta}{8+2\gamma}}.

By applying (
3.5
), (
3.6
) and the following classical inequality

a_{1}^{r}+a_{2}^{r}\leq(a_{1}+a_{2})^{r},a_{1},a_{2}\geq 0,r\geq 1,

we infer that

\displaystyle\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptp}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptp}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-49.08328pt\hskip 49.08328ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143ptp}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.73143pt\hskip 33.73143ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|F(t,u,y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375ptp}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.11375pt\hskip 30.11375ptH}}}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}(1+\lvert u\rvert^{2}_{H})^{\frac{p(1-\theta)}{2}}(1+\lvert u\rvert^{8+2\gamma}_{H})^{\frac{p\theta}{8+2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}(1+\lvert u\rvert_{H})^{p(1-\theta)}(1+\lvert u\rvert_{H})^{p\theta}\leq C_{p}(1+\lvert u\rvert^{p}_{H}).

We now state an important remark on the Assumptions F.
3
and F.
4
.

Remark 3.3
.

Assumptions F.
3
and F.
4
look very similar. However, let us point out that

Assumption F.
3
is the continuity of some map with values in
H_{w}
;

Assumption F.
4
is the continuity of some map with values in
U^{\prime}
;

H_{w}
may not be continuously embedded into
U^{\prime}
and therefore Assumption F.
3
does not in general imply F.
4
.

Before we continue let us formulate a result that will be useful in examples of maps satisfying Assumptions F.
1
-F.
5
.

Lemma 3.4
.

If
u_{n}\to u
in
\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})
then

\int_{0}^{T}|u_{n}(t)-u(t)|_{U^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\to 0
.

Proof.

Note that if
u_{n}\to u
in
\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})
, then
u_{n}(t)\to u(t)
in
U^{\prime}
for Leb-a.a.
t\in[0,T]
. Observe also that by
[
14
, Lemma 2]

the assumption implies that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lvert u_{n}(t)\rvert_{U^{\prime}}^{2}<\infty.

Hence, by the Lebesgue Dominated Convergence Theorem we infer that

\int_{0}^{T}|u_{n}(t)-u_{n}(t)|_{U^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\to 0.

∎

Example 3.5
.

A particularly important example of the noise coefficient
F
is when it is linear with respect to
u
. We can then write

F(t,u,y)=G(t,y)u,\mbox{ for appropriate }(t,u,y).

(3.9)

In this framework, our Assumptions take the following simpler form.

For every

(t,y)\in[0,T]\times Y
,

G(t,y)
is a bounded linear map from
U^{\prime}
to
U^{\prime}
and from
H
to
H
. Moreover, the associated maps, denoted by the same symbols,

G\colon[0,T]\times Y\to\mathscr{L}(U^{\prime})\cap\mathscr{L}(H)

are strongly measurable, i.e. for all
u^{\prime}\in U^{\prime}
and
u\in H
, the maps

G(\cdot,\cdot)u^{\prime}\colon[0,T]\times Y\ni(t,y)\mapsto G(t,y)u^{\prime}\in U^{\prime}

and

G(\cdot,\cdot)u\colon[0,T]\times Y\ni(t,y)\mapsto G(t,y)u\in H

are measurable.

There exists a constant
C_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.54556pt\hskip 31.54556pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.54556pt\hskip 31.54556ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.21512pt\hskip 28.21512pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.21512pt\hskip 28.21512ptH}}}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq{C}_{3}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.47806pt\hskip 9.47806pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.47806pt\hskip 9.47806ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81377pt\hskip 8.81377pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81377pt\hskip 8.81377ptH}}},\;u\in H,\;t\in[0,T].

(3.10)

For every
\phi\in\mathscr{V}
the associated Nemytski map
{\tilde{G}}_{\phi}
defined by

\displaystyle{\tilde{G}}_{\phi}

\displaystyle\colon\mathscr{Z}_{T}\ni u\mapsto\bigl\{[0,T]\times Y\ni(t,y)\mapsto{\bigl(G(t,y)u(t),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\in{L}^{2}([0,T]\times Y,\Leb\otimes\nu;\mathbb{R})

is continuous.

The linear map

\displaystyle\textbf{G}\colon\mathscr{Z}_{T}\ni u\mapsto\bigl\{[0,T]\times Y\ni(t,y)

\displaystyle\mapsto G(t,y)u(t)\in U^{\prime}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\in{L}^{2}([0,T]\times Y,\Leb\otimes\nu,U^{\prime})\quad

is continuous.

There exist a number
\gamma>0
and a constant
{C}_{\gamma}>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.54556pt\hskip 31.54556pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.54556pt\hskip 31.54556ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.21512pt\hskip 28.21512pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.21512pt\hskip 28.21512ptH}}}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq{C}_{\gamma}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.47806pt\hskip 9.47806pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.47806pt\hskip 9.47806ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81377pt\hskip 8.81377pt8+2\gamma}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81377pt\hskip 8.81377ptH}}},\;u\in H,\;t\in[0,T].

(3.11)

Remark 3.6
.

A natural question arises whether Assumptions (G4) and (G5) follow from (G1-2).
The simplest example of function
G
is the following one

G(t,y)=\mathbf{G},\mbox{ for all }(t,y)\in[0,T]\times Y,

where
\mathbf{G}\in\mathscr{L}(U^{\prime})\cap\mathscr{L}(H)
.

If additionally
\mathbf{G}
maps
\mathscr{V}
into itself, then Assumption (G4) follows from Assumptions (G1-2). Indeed, we have

{\bigl(G(t,y)u(t),\phi\bigr)}_{H}={\bigl(u(t),G(t,y)^{\ast}\phi\bigr)}_{H},\mbox{ for all }(t,y)\in[0,T]\times Y,\;\;\phi\in\mathscr{V}.

We also have the following result which implies that condition (
3.12
) is sufficient for assumption (
G
.5) to hold.

Lemma 3.7
.

Assume that the map
G
satisfies assumptions (G1-2) and the following modification of (G3):

There exists a constant
C_{3}^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.6819pt\hskip 44.6819ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.54556pt\hskip 31.54556pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.54556pt\hskip 31.54556ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|G(t,y)u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.21512pt\hskip 28.21512pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.21512pt\hskip 28.21512ptU^{\prime}}}}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq{C}_{3}^{\prime}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.72449pt\hskip 15.72449ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.47806pt\hskip 9.47806pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.47806pt\hskip 9.47806ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|u|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81377pt\hskip 8.81377pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81377pt\hskip 8.81377ptU^{\prime}}}},\;u\in U^{\prime},\;t\in[0,T].

(3.12)

Then

the linear map

\displaystyle\textbf{G}\colon\mathscr{Z}_{T}\ni u\mapsto\bigl\{[0,T]\times Y\ni(t,y)

\displaystyle\mapsto G(t,y)u(t)\in U^{\prime}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\in{L}^{2}([0,T]\times Y,\Leb\otimes\nu,U^{\prime})\quad

is continuous with respect to the
\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})
topology.

Proof of Lemma
3.7
.

Let us assume that
(u_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is an
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
-valued sequence such that
u_{n}\to u
in the topology of
\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})
.
Then, we have the following chain of inequalities

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{Y}|G(t,y)u_{n}(t)-G(t,y)u(t)|_{U^{\prime}}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{Y}|G(t,y)\left(u_{n}(t)-u(t)\right)|_{U^{\prime}}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq{C}_{3}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{T}|u_{n}(t)-u(t)|_{U^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

Thus, by Lemma
3.4
we conclude the proof of the Lemma.
∎

Before we proceed further let us give another example of a map
F
satisfying Assumptions F.
1
-F.
5
.
This example is related to Example
3.5
.

Example 3.8
.

Consider a one dimensional Lévy process
L
and the corresponding PRM
\eta
. Then, we have
Y=\mathbb{R}
which is a standard measurable space. Furthermore, we assume that the Lévy measure
\nu
of
\eta
satisfies
\nu(\{0\})=0
and that there exists a number
\gamma>0
such that
for every
p\in\{2,8+2\gamma\}

\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(-1,1)}|y|^{p}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)<\infty.

Let
F
be the bilinear multiplicative map defined by

F(t,u,y)\coloneqq uy,\;\;(t,u,y)\in[0,T]\times U^{\prime}\times\mathbb{R}.

It is clear that
F
satisfies Assumption F.
1
. Since
\nu(\{0\})=0
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}|y|^{p}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)<\infty
it is not difficult to show that
F
satisfies the Assumptions F.
2
-F.
3
and Assumption F.
5
.
What only remains to do is to verify whether
F
satisfies Assumption F.
4
.

For this aim, let
(u_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathscr{Z}_{T}
such that
u_{n}\to u
in the topology of
\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})
.
Then, we have the following chain of inequalities

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{Y}|F(t,u_{n}(t),y)-F(t,u(t),y)|_{U^{\prime}}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|u_{n}(t)y-u(t)y|_{U^{\prime}}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|u_{n}(t)-u(t)|_{U^{\prime}}^{2}y^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\int_{0}^{T}|u_{n}(t)-u(t)|_{U^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t.

Lemma
3.4
implies that

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{Y}|F(t,u_{n}(t),y)-F(t,u(t),y)|_{U^{\prime}}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}t\to 0,

as
n\to\infty

because
\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)<\infty
and
\int_{0}^{T}|u_{n}(t)-u_{n}(t)|_{U^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\to 0
since

u_{n}\to u
in
\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})
.

3.2
Definitions and the main result

We now continue with the concept of solutions of the Problem (
3.1
) and the statement of the main result of this paper.

Definition 3.9
.

A martingale solution
of equation (
3.1
)
is a system

\bigl(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{F},\mathbb{P},u,\eta\bigr),

where

\bullet

\bigl(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{F},\mathbb{P}\bigr)
is a complete filtered probability space with a filtration
\mathbb{F}=\left(\mathscr{F}_{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
, satisfying the usual assumption,

\bullet

\eta
is a time homogeneous Poisson random measure on
(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times Y,\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\otimes\mathscr{Y})
over

(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{F},\mathbb{P})
with
\sigma
-finite intensity measure
\Leb\otimes\nu
and the corresponding compensated Poisson random measure
\tilde{\eta}
.

\bullet

u\colon[0,T]\times\Omega\to{H}
is a predictable process with
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. paths

\displaystyle u(\cdot,\omega)\in\mathbb{D}\bigl([0,T];{H}_{w}\bigr)\cap{L}^{2}(0,T;V)

such that for all
t\in[0,T]
the following identity holds
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
v\in V

\displaystyle{\bigl(u(t),v\bigr)}_{H}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip 9.14935pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle\mathscr{A}u(s),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.11812pt\hskip 43.11812pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-9.14935pt\hskip-43.11812ptV^{\prime}\hskip 43.11812ptV}}}{{\hskip 9.14935pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle\mathscr{A}u(s),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.11812pt\hskip 43.11812pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-9.14935pt\hskip-43.11812ptV^{\prime}\hskip 43.11812ptV}}}{{\hskip 8.23274pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle\mathscr{A}u(s),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.94812pt\hskip 34.94812pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-8.23274pt\hskip-34.94812ptV^{\prime}\hskip 34.94812ptV}}}{{\hskip 8.23274pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle\mathscr{A}u(s),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.48294pt\hskip 31.48294pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-8.23274pt\hskip-31.48294ptV^{\prime}\hskip 31.48294ptV}}}{}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip 9.14935pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B(u(s)),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-51.48271pt\hskip 51.48271pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-9.14935pt\hskip-51.48271ptV^{\prime}\hskip 51.48271ptV}}}{{\hskip 9.14935pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B(u(s)),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-51.48271pt\hskip 51.48271pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-9.14935pt\hskip-51.48271ptV^{\prime}\hskip 51.48271ptV}}}{{\hskip 8.23274pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B(u(s)),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.67833pt\hskip 41.67833pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-8.23274pt\hskip-41.67833ptV^{\prime}\hskip 41.67833ptV}}}{{\hskip 8.23274pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B(u(s)),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-37.41525pt\hskip 37.41525pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-8.23274pt\hskip-37.41525ptV^{\prime}\hskip 37.41525ptV}}}{}\,\mathrm{d}s={\bigl({u}_{0},v\bigr)}_{H}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip 9.14935pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle f(s),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.86578pt\hskip 35.86578pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-9.14935pt\hskip-35.86578ptV^{\prime}\hskip 35.86578ptV}}}{{\hskip 9.14935pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle f(s),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.86578pt\hskip 35.86578pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-9.14935pt\hskip-35.86578ptV^{\prime}\hskip 35.86578ptV}}}{{\hskip 8.23274pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle f(s),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.00133pt\hskip 29.00133pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-8.23274pt\hskip-29.00133ptV^{\prime}\hskip 29.00133ptV}}}{{\hskip 8.23274pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle f(s),v\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-26.36014pt\hskip 26.36014pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-8.23274pt\hskip-26.36014ptV^{\prime}\hskip 26.36014ptV}}}{}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,u(s-),y),v\bigr)}_{H}\,\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y).

Remark 3.10
.

According to our proof the
\mathbb{P}
-full set does not depend on
v\in V
.

Note that the spaces
U
and
U^{\prime}
are purely auxiliary and do no appear in the definition above.

Theorem 3.11
.

There exists a martingale solution of the problem (
3.1
) provided assumptions A.
1
, P.
1
and F.
1
-F.
5

are satisfied.

The next two sections contain numerous intermediate results needed in the proof of this theorem which will be given in Section
6
.

4
Galerkin approximation

In this section we construct a system of
n
stochastic differential equations with globally Lipschitz coefficients on a finite dimensional space
H_{n}\subset H
for arbitrary
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We will also derive uniform estimates for the soltuions of these SDEs.
In all our results in this section we require Assumption F.
2
.
Whenever we require Assumption F.
5
we mention this explicitly.

4.1
Faedo-Galerkin approximation

Thanks to
[
34
, Theorem I.8.1]
we find a time homogeneous PRM
\eta
on
(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times Y,\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\times\mathscr{Y})
such that its intensity measure is
\Leb\otimes\nu
and constructed on a probability space
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P})
. Denote by
\mathscr{N}
the collection of all subsets of the null sets in
\mathscr{F}
.
The compensated PRM corresponding to
\eta
is denoted by
\tilde{\eta}
. We equipped the probability space with the following filtration

\displaystyle{\mathbb{F}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq({{\mathscr{F}}}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]},

\displaystyle\mbox{ where }

\displaystyle{{\displaystyle\mathscr{F}}}_{t}=\sigma\bigl(\mathscr{H}_{t+}\cup\mathscr{N}\bigr)

\displaystyle\mbox{ and }

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}_{t+}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigcap_{s>t}\mathscr{H}_{s}\mbox{ with }\mathscr{H}_{s}\coloneqq\sigma\{\eta([0,r]\times A):\,\,r\leq s,\,A\in\mathscr{Y}\}.

Note that the filtration
{\mathbb{F}}
satisfies the usual conditions and
\eta
is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted.

Now, we consider the following Faedo-Galerkin approximation of the Problem (
3.1
)
in the space
{H}_{n}

\displaystyle{u}_{n}(t)

\displaystyle={P}_{n}{u}_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\bigl({P}_{n}\mathscr{A}{u}_{n}(s)+{B}_{n}\bigl({u}_{n}(s)\bigr)-{P}_{n}f(s)\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.1)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{P}_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}({s}{-}),y)\,\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y),\quad t\in[0,T].

It is not difficult to prove that the map
{B}_{n}\colon{H}_{n}\to{H}_{n}
is globally Lipschitz continuous.
Thus, thanks to the linearity of
\mathscr{A}_{n}
and (
3.4
) in Assumption F.
2
we see that (
4.1
) is a system of stochastic differential equations with globally Lipschitz coefficients. Thus, by applying
[
34
, Theorem 9.1]
we obtain the following existence result.

Lemma 4.1

(
[
47
]
, Lemma 3)
.

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, equation (
4.1
) has a unique global
\mathbb{F}
-adapted, càdlàg
{H}_{n}
valued solution
{u}_{n}
.

Now,
by employing equalities (
2.57
) and (
2.58
)
the following three lemmata can be established with exactly the same proofs as in
[
47
, Lemma 3 and 4]
which hold provided the noise coefficient
F
satisfies condition (
3.5
) in Assumption
2
and Assumption F.
5
. .

Lemma
4.2
is proved by using the finite dimensional Itô formula, see
[
34
]
or
[
45
]
,
and the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy (BDG) inequality, see
[
38
, Theorem 26.12]
.

Lemma 4.2

(
[
47
]
, Lemma 4)
.

The processes
({u}_{n}{)}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
from Lemma
4.1
satisfy the following conditions.

There exists a positive constant
{C}_{2}>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095ptV}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095ptV}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.4064pt\hskip 24.4064pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.4064pt\hskip 24.4064ptV}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.4852pt\hskip 22.4852pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.4852pt\hskip 22.4852ptV}}}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]\leq{C}_{2},\;\;\mbox{ for every }n\in\mathbb{N},.

(4.2)

There exists a positive constant
{C}_{1}
such that every
p\in[1,4+\gamma]

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095ptp}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095ptp}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.4064pt\hskip 24.4064ptp}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.4064pt\hskip 24.4064ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.4852pt\hskip 22.4852ptp}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.4852pt\hskip 22.4852ptH}}}\right]\leq{C}_{1}^{\frac{p}{4+\gamma}},\;\;\mbox{ for every }n\in\mathbb{N}.

(4.3)

Note that it is sufficient to prove assertion (
4.3
) only for
p=4+\gamma
. The case
p<4+\gamma
follows from the former by the Jensen inequality.
We recall the proof in Appendix
G
.

4.2
Tightness

As is Section
2
we chose and fix
s>\frac{d}{2}+1
. Let us recall that the space

{V}_{s}
defined in identity (
2.17
)
is dense in
V
and the embedding
{j}_{s}\colon{V}_{s}\hookrightarrow V
is continuous. Let us also recall that the Hilbert space
U
introduced at the end of the Section
2
is a dense subset of
{V}_{s}
and as mentioned in (
2.23
) the natural embedding
{\iota}_{s}\colon U\hookrightarrow{V}_{s}
is compact.

With this in mind, we will apply a standard tightness condition
[
47
, Corollary 1]
with
q=2
.

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the solution
{u}_{n}
of the Galerkin equation defines a measure

\mathcal{L}({u}_{n})
on
(\mathscr{Z}_{T},\mathcal{T})
, where the space
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
has been defined in (
3.3
).
Using
[
47
, Corollary 1]
, with
q=2
, one can prove that the set of measures

\bigl\{\mathcal{L}({u}_{n}):n\in\mathbb{N}\bigr\}
is tight on
(\mathscr{Z}_{T},\mathcal{T})
. The inequalities (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) with
p=2
in Lemma
4.2
are of crucial importance.

Lemma 4.3
.

The set of measures
\bigl\{\mathcal{L}({u}_{n}):n\in\mathbb{N}\bigr\}
is tight on
(\mathscr{Z}_{T},\mathcal{T})
.

Proof.

This lemma can be proved with the same approach as used in
[
47
, Lem. 5]
, thus we omit the proof. Let us point out that as it is explicitely visible from the proof of Theorem 2 therein, one needs assumption (
2.34
), i.e. that the embedding
H\hookrightarrow U^{\prime}
is compact.
∎

4.3
Application of the Skorohod-Jakubowski Theorem

We will use the following Jakubowski’s version of the Skorokhod Theorem in the form given by
Brzeźniak and Ondreját
[
18
]
, see also Jakubowski
[
37
]
. The version below does not use almost sure convergence of

[
18
]
but a stronger pointwise convergence as in
[
37
, Theorem 2]
.

Theorem 4.4
.

(Theorem A.1 in
[
18
]
)

Let
\mathscr{X}
be a topological space such that there exists a sequence
\{{f}_{m}\}
of continuous functions
{f}_{m}\colon\mathscr{X}\to\mathbb{R}
that separates points of
\mathscr{X}
. Let us denote by
\mathcal{S}
the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the maps
\{{f}_{m}\}
.
Then

(j1)

every compact subset of
\mathscr{X}
is metrizable;

(j2)

if
({\mu}_{m})
is a tight sequence of probability measures on
(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{S})
, then there exists a subsequence
({m}_{k})
, a probability space
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P})
with
\mathscr{X}
-valued Borel measurable variables
{\xi}_{k},\xi
such that
{\mu}_{{m}_{k}}
is the law of
{\xi}_{k}

and
{\xi}_{k}
converges to
\xi
on
\Omega
.
Moreover, the law of
\xi
is a Radon measure.

Using Theorem
4.4
, we obtain the following corollary which we will apply to construct a martingale solution to the Navier-Stokes equations.

Corollary 4.5
.

Let
({y}_{n}{)}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
-valued random variables such that their laws
\mathcal{L}({y}_{n})
on
(\mathscr{Z}_{T},\mathcal{T})
form a tight sequence of probability measures.
Then there exists a subsequence
({n}_{k})
, a probability space
(\overline{\Omega},\overline{\mathscr{F}},\overline{\mathbb{P}})
and
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
-valued random variables
\tilde{y},{\tilde{y}}_{n_{k}}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that the variables
{y}_{n_{k}}

and
{\tilde{y}}_{n_{k}}
have the same laws on
\mathscr{Z}_{T}

and
{\tilde{y}}_{n_{k}}
converges to
\tilde{y}
on
\overline{\Omega}
.

Proof.

It is proven in
[
47
, Remark 2, p. 905]
that the space
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
4.4
, thus the assertions of the Corollary follows from Theorem
4.4
-(j2).
∎

Now, owing to Lemma
4.3
and Corollary
4.5
we can find
a probability space

\bigl(\overline{\Omega},\overline{\mathscr{F}},\overline{\mathbb{P}}\bigr),

(4.4)

an increasing sequence
({n}_{k}{)}_{k}
of natural numbers and

\mathscr{Z}_{T}
-valued random variables
\overline{u}
,
{\overline{u}}_{{n}_{k}}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}

defined on
\bigl(\overline{\Omega},\overline{\mathscr{F}},\overline{\mathbb{P}}\bigr)
, such that
{\overline{u}}_{{n}_{k}}
has the same law as
{u}_{{n}_{k}}
on
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
and

{\overline{u}}_{{n}_{k}}\to\overline{u}\mbox{ in }\mathscr{Z}_{T}\quad{\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}.}

(4.5)

Without loss of generality, we can and will denote the subsequence
({\overline{u}}_{{n}_{k}})_{k}
by
({\overline{u}}_{n})_{n}
or simply by
({\overline{u}}_{n})
. In view of Definition
3.3
of the space
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
, the above assertion (
4.5
) is equivalent to the following assertions:

\displaystyle{\overline{u}}_{n}\to\overline{u}\mbox{ in }{L}_{w}^{2}(0,T;V)\quad{\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}},

(4.6)

\displaystyle{\overline{u}}_{n}\to\overline{u}\mbox{ in }{L}^{2}(0,T;{H}_{\mathrm{loc}})\quad{\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}},

(4.7)

\displaystyle{\overline{u}}_{n}\to\overline{u}\mbox{ in }\mathbb{D}([0,T];{H}_{w})\quad{\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}},

(4.8)

\displaystyle{\overline{u}}_{n}\to\overline{u}\mbox{ in }\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})\quad{\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}}.

(4.9)

To close this subsection we state the following important remark.

Remark 4.6
.

It follows
from the definition (
3.3
) of the space
\mathscr{Z}_{T}={L}_{w}^{2}(0,T;V)\cap{L}^{2}(0,T;{H}_{\mathrm{loc}})\cap\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})\cap\mathbb{D}([0,T];{H}_{w})

that
the
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
-valued random variables
\overline{u}
defined on
\bigl(\overline{\Omega},\overline{\mathscr{F}},\overline{\mathbb{P}}\bigr)
induces
a measurable process (denoted by the same letter)

\overline{u}\colon[0,T]\times\overline{\Omega}\to V\cap{H}_{\mathrm{loc}},

whose trajectories belong to the space

\mathscr{Z}_{T}
, see e.g.
[
22
, Proposition B.1]
.

4.4
A priori estimates for the processes on the new probability space

In the earlier parts of the paper we viewed
\overline{u}
as a
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
-valued random variable, but from now we treat it as an
U^{\prime}
-valued process, which can be done as in Remark
4.6
, see also
[
22
, Proposition B.1]
. Moreover, in view of the Kuratowski Theorem, we can view it as an
H
-valued process as well and
as an important consequence of (
4.8
) and
[
14
, Lemma 2]

the trajectories of the sequence of processes
({\overline{u}}_{n})
are uniformly bounded in
H
, i.e. they satisfy the following condition

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(t)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.61pt\hskip 29.61pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.61pt\hskip 29.61ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(t)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.61pt\hskip 29.61pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.61pt\hskip 29.61ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(t)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.92488pt\hskip 23.92488pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.92488pt\hskip 23.92488ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(t)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.72241pt\hskip 22.72241pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.72241pt\hskip 22.72241ptH}}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|\overline{u}(t)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.16667pt\hskip 24.16667pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.16667pt\hskip 24.16667ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|\overline{u}(t)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.16667pt\hskip 24.16667pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.16667pt\hskip 24.16667ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|\overline{u}(t)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.02089pt\hskip 19.02089pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.02089pt\hskip 19.02089ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|\overline{u}(t)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.81842pt\hskip 17.81842pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.81842pt\hskip 17.81842ptH}}}<\infty,\;\;\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}.

(4.10)

This observation will be crucial in our argument below. Since every weakly convergent sequence is bounded we infer from (
4.6
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.99892pt\hskip 15.99892pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.99892pt\hskip 15.99892pt{L}^{2}(0,T;V)}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.99892pt\hskip 15.99892pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.99892pt\hskip 15.99892pt{L}^{2}(0,T;V)}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-14.654pt\hskip 14.654pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-14.654pt\hskip 14.654pt{L}^{2}(0,T;V)}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-14.48738pt\hskip 14.48738pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-14.48738pt\hskip 14.48738pt{L}^{2}(0,T;V)}}}+\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|\overline{u}|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.55559pt\hskip 10.55559pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.55559pt\hskip 10.55559pt{L}^{2}(0,T;V)}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|\overline{u}|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.55559pt\hskip 10.55559pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.55559pt\hskip 10.55559pt{L}^{2}(0,T;V)}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|\overline{u}|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.75002pt\hskip 9.75002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.75002pt\hskip 9.75002pt{L}^{2}(0,T;V)}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|\overline{u}|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.58339pt\hskip 9.58339pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.58339pt\hskip 9.58339pt{L}^{2}(0,T;V)}}}<\infty,\;\;\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}.

(4.11)

We finish these preliminary comments on the conclusions from the Skorohod Theorem by the following result.

Lemma 4.7
.

The following two assertions hold on
\overline{\Omega}

\displaystyle{\overline{u}}_{n}\to\overline{u}\mbox{ \it in }{L}_{w}^{2}(0,T;{H})

(4.12)

and, for every
v\in L^{2}(0,T;V)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}{\bigl(\nabla{\overline{u}}_{n}(s),\nabla v(s)\bigr)}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\to\int_{0}^{T}{\bigl(\nabla\overline{u}(s),\nabla v(s)\bigr)}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s.

(4.13)

Proof of Lemma
4.7
.

Let us choose and fix an arbitrary
\omega\in\overline{\Omega}
. Then, by (
4.6
),
{\overline{u}}_{n}={\overline{u}}_{n}(\omega)\to\overline{u}=\overline{u}(\omega)
weakly in
L^{2}(0,T;V)
.
Since the natural embedding
i\colon L^{2}(0,T;V)\hookrightarrow L^{2}(0,T;{H})
is linear and bounded, its adjoint
i^{\ast}\colon L^{2}(0,T;{H})\to L^{2}(0,T;V)
is linear and bounded as well. Hence we infer that
{\overline{u}}_{n}\to\overline{u}
weakly in
L^{2}(0,T;{H})
.

The second claim follows by observing that for every
v\in L^{2}(0,T;V)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}{\bigl(\nabla\overline{u}_{n}(s),\nabla v(s)\bigr)}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s=\int_{0}^{T}{\bigl(\overline{u}_{n}(s),v(s)\bigr)}_{V}-{\bigl(\overline{u}_{n}(s),v(s)\bigr)}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s,

from which along with (
4.6
) and (
4.12
) we see that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}{\bigl(\nabla\overline{u}_{n}(s),\nabla v(s)\bigr)}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\to\int_{0}^{T}{\bigl(\overline{u}(s),v(s)\bigr)}_{V}-{\bigl(\overline{u}(s),v(s)\bigr)}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{0}^{T}{\bigl(\nabla\overline{u}(s),\nabla v(s)\bigr)}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s.

∎

We also have the following estimates.

Lemma 4.8
.

(i)

\overline{u}_{n}
is almost surely in
\mathbb{D}([0,T];P_{n}H)
and for any
p\in[2,4+\gamma)
there exists a constant
C_{1}(p)>0

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\bigl|{\overline{u}}_{n}(s){\bigr|}_{H}^{p}\right]\leq{C}_{1}(p),

(4.14)

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}{\bigl\|{\overline{u}}_{n}(s)\bigr\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]\leq{C}_{2}.

(4.15)

(ii)

\overline{u}
satisfies the following estimates

\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\overline{u}(s)\rVert_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]<\infty,

(4.16)

\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\bigl|\overline{u}(s){\bigr|}_{H}^{2}\right]<\infty.

(4.17)

Proof.

(i) Let us observe that by the Kuratowski Theorem, see
[
52
, Theorem I.3.9]
,

\mathbb{D}([0,T];{P}_{n}H)
is a Borel subset of
\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})\cap{L}^{2}(0,T;{H}_{\mathrm{loc}})
. Indeed, both spaces
\mathbb{D}([0,T];{P}_{n}H)
and
\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})\cap{L}^{2}(0,T;{H}_{\mathrm{loc}})
are Polish and the natural embedding of the former into the latter is continuous (and hence Borel measurable) and obviously injective.
Since
{u}_{n}\in\mathbb{D}([0,T];{P}_{n}H)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. and
{\overline{u}}_{n}
and
{u}_{n}
have the same laws, we infer that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\overline{u}}_{n})\bigl(\mathbb{D}([0,T];{P}_{n}H)\bigr)=1,\qquad n\geq 1.

Firstly, note that the function

\mathscr{Z}_{T}\ni u\mapsto\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\bigl|u(s){\bigr|}_{H}^{p}

(4.18)

is measurable.
Indeed, similarly to
[
9
, Appendix E]
, it is enough to show that the function (
4.18
) is lower semicontinuous.
This follows from the fact that the set

\{u\in D([0,T],H_{w}):\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lvert u(t)\right\rvert_{H}\leq r\}

is closed in
D([0,T],H_{w})
, see
[
36
, Proposition 1.6(v)]
.

The function

\mathscr{Z}_{T}\ni u\mapsto\int_{0}^{T}{\bigl\|u(s)\bigr\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.19)

is also measurable since it is
\mathscr{B}(L_{w}^{2}(0,T;V))/\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})
measurable. Indeed, it is lower-semicontinuous in view of Corolaries to
[
55
, Theorem 3.12]
.

Since
{\overline{u}}_{n}
and
{u}_{n}
have the same laws, and
\mathbb{D}([0,T];{P}_{n}H)
is a Borel subset of

\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})\cap{L}^{2}(0,T;{H}_{\mathrm{loc}})
, thus by (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) with
p=2
we obtain the estimates (
4.14
) and (
4.15
).

(ii) By the Banach-Alaoglu Theorem and inequality (
4.15
) we infer that the sequence
({\overline{u}}_{n})
contains subsequence, still denoted by
({\overline{u}}_{n})
, which is weakly convergent in the space
{L}^{2}([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega};V)
.
Since by (
4.5
)
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.
{\overline{u}}_{n}\to\overline{u}
in
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
, we conclude that
{\overline{u}}_{n}\to\overline{u}
weakly in
{L}^{2}([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega};V)
and that

\overline{u}\in{L}^{2}([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega};V)
, i.e. (
4.16
) holds.

Similarly, by the Banach-Alaoglu Theorem and inequality (
4.14
) with
p=2
we can choose a subsequence of
({\overline{u}}_{n})
, still denoted by
({\overline{u}}_{n})
, which is weakly-star convergent in the space
{L}^{2}(\overline{\Omega};{L}^{\infty}(0,T;{H}))
.
Since by (
4.8
)
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.
{\overline{u}}_{n}\to\overline{u}
in
\mathbb{D}([0,T];{H}_{w})
, we infer that
{\overline{u}}_{n}\to\overline{u}
weakly-star in
{L}^{2}(\overline{\Omega};{L}^{\infty}(0,T;{H}))
and that (
4.17
) holds.
∎

5
Auxiliary Martingales

In this section, whenever we use Assumption F.
5
, we specify this explicitly. The main results in this section are Lemmata
5.10
and
5.11
.

Let us recall, see Lemma
4.1
, that for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the process
{u}_{n}
is the unique
\mathbb{F}
-adapted, càdlàg
{H}_{n}
-valued solution to the Galerkin approximation equation (
4.1
).

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\phi\in U
let us define the following two
{H}_{n}
-, and respectively,
\mathbb{R}
-valued processes
{{M}}_{n}
and
N_{n,\phi}
, on the original probability space
\bigl({\Omega},\mathscr{F},{\mathbb{P}},{\mathbb{F}}\bigr)
, by, for
t\in[0,T]
,

{{M}}_{n}(t)\coloneqq u_{n}(t)-u_{n}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}{P}_{n}\mathscr{A}u_{n}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}B_{n}\bigl(u_{n}(s)\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}s-\int_{0}^{t}{P}_{n}f(s)\,\mathrm{d}s

(5.1)

and

\displaystyle N_{n,\phi}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\langle u_{n}(t),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.73738pt\hskip 40.73738pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-40.73738ptU^{\prime}\hskip 40.73738ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\langle u_{n}(t),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.73738pt\hskip 40.73738pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-40.73738ptU^{\prime}\hskip 40.73738ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\langle u_{n}(t),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.75296pt\hskip 33.75296pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-33.75296ptU^{\prime}\hskip 33.75296ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\langle u_{n}(t),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.3695pt\hskip 31.3695pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-31.3695ptU^{\prime}\hskip 31.3695ptU}}}-\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\langle u_{n}(0),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-42.12628pt\hskip 42.12628pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-42.12628ptU^{\prime}\hskip 42.12628ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\langle u_{n}(0),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-42.12628pt\hskip 42.12628pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-42.12628ptU^{\prime}\hskip 42.12628ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\langle u_{n}(0),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.71825pt\hskip 34.71825pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-34.71825ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.71825ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\langle u_{n}(0),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.95403pt\hskip 31.95403pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-31.95403ptU^{\prime}\hskip 31.95403ptU}}}

\displaystyle+2\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle{P}_{n}\mathscr{A}u_{n}u(s)\,+B_{n}(u_{n}(s)){-P_{n}f(s)},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-163.86609pt\hskip 163.86609pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-163.86609ptU^{\prime}\hskip 163.86609ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle{P}_{n}\mathscr{A}u_{n}u(s)\,+B_{n}(u_{n}(s)){-P_{n}f(s)},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-163.86609pt\hskip 163.86609pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-163.86609ptU^{\prime}\hskip 163.86609ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle{P}_{n}\mathscr{A}u_{n}u(s)\,+B_{n}(u_{n}(s)){-P_{n}f(s)},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.84926pt\hskip 128.84926pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-128.84926ptU^{\prime}\hskip 128.84926ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle{P}_{n}\mathscr{A}u_{n}u(s)\,+B_{n}(u_{n}(s)){-P_{n}f(s)},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-116.08516pt\hskip 116.08516pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-116.08516ptU^{\prime}\hskip 116.08516ptU}}}\;\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.81377pt\hskip 41.81377pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.81377ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.81377ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.81377pt\hskip 41.81377pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.81377ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.81377ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.50644pt\hskip 34.50644pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-34.50644ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.50644ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.90192pt\hskip 31.90192pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-31.90192ptU^{\prime}\hskip 31.90192ptU}}}\,\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\langle{P}_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y),\phi\rangle^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s,\quad t\in[0,T].

(5.2)

It follows from identity (
4.1
) that

{{M}}_{n}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{P}_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}({s}{-}),y)\;\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y),\;\;\;t\in[0,T].

(5.3)

Lemma 5.1
.

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the process
{{M}}_{n}
is a square integrable
\mathbb{F}
-martingale and

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigl|{{M}}_{n}(t)\bigr|_{H}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{Y}\rvert P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)|_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr]

(5.4)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left(1+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lvert u_{n}(t)\rvert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\right)<\infty.

(5.5)

Moreover, if Assumption F.
5
is satisfied, then for every
p\in[2,4+\gamma]
and for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the process
{{M}}_{n}
is a
p
-integrable
\mathbb{F}
-martingale

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigl|{{M}}_{n}(t)\bigr|_{H}^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\;\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{Y}\rvert P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)|_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr)^{\frac{p}{2}}

(5.6)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{Y}\rvert P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)|_{H}^{p}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left(1+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\lvert u_{n}(t)\rvert^{p}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\right)<\infty.

(5.7)

The constants
C_{2}
,
C_{p}
are generic positive constants which can change from line to line but which do not depend on
n
.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
. Part I.

Let us first observe that because of (
5.3
), the process
M_{n}
is a square-integrable
H
-valued martingale. Thus inequality
(
5.4
)
follows from the BDG inequality, see
[
45
, Theorem 20.6]
.
The first inequality in (
5.5
) follows by applying
the linear growth condition (
3.5
) in Assumption F.
2
.
The second inequality in (
5.5
) follows from inequality (
4.3
) with
p=2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.1
; part II.

Let us choose and fix
p\in(2,4+\gamma]
.
The first part of inequality (
5.6
) follows from inequality (2.32) in
[
68
, Theorem 2.6]
. Inequality (
5.7
) is a consequence of (
5.6
) and the linear growth assumption (
3.6
) in Assumption
5
.
The second part of inequality (
5.7
) follows from the first one and inequality (
4.3
) in Lemma
4.2
.
∎

\displaystyle\overline{N}_{n,\phi}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\langle{\overline{u}}_{n}(t),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.01282pt\hskip 40.01282pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-40.01282ptU^{\prime}\hskip 40.01282ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\langle{\overline{u}}_{n}(t),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.01282pt\hskip 40.01282pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-40.01282ptU^{\prime}\hskip 40.01282ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\langle{\overline{u}}_{n}(t),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.02492pt\hskip 34.02492pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-34.02492ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.02492ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\langle{\overline{u}}_{n}(t),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.13913pt\hskip 32.13913pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-32.13913ptU^{\prime}\hskip 32.13913ptU}}}-\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\langle\overline{u}_{n}(0),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.40172pt\hskip 41.40172pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.40172ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.40172ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\langle\overline{u}_{n}(0),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.40172pt\hskip 41.40172pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.40172ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.40172ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\langle\overline{u}_{n}(0),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.9902pt\hskip 34.9902pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-34.9902ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.9902ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\langle\overline{u}_{n}(0),\phi\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.72365pt\hskip 32.72365pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-32.72365ptU^{\prime}\hskip 32.72365ptU}}}

\displaystyle+2\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip Since
H_{n}
is finite dimensional and these processes are càdlàg in
H
equipped with the weak topology, it follows that

and

. Consequently,
\overline{N}_{n,\phi}
has trajectories in

.

Also, since
{\overline{u}}_{n}
has trajectories in
\mathbb{D}([0,T];H_{n})
and
\phi\in U\subset H
, by (
2.31
) we have

\displaystyle\overline{N}_{n,\phi}(t)

\displaystyle={\bigl({\overline{u}}_{n}(t),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}-{\bigl(\overline{u}_{n}(0),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle+2\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle{P}_{n}\mathscr{A}{\overline{u}}_{n}u(s)+B_{n}({\overline{u}}_{n}(s)),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip
