\displaystyle+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.68639pt\hskip 30.68639pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.68639pt\hskip 30.68639ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.68639pt\hskip 30.68639pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.68639pt\hskip 30.68639ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.67836pt\hskip 24.67836pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.67836pt\hskip 24.67836ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.25482pt\hskip 23.25482pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.25482pt\hskip 23.25482ptH}}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(s)-\overline{u}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-53.7072pt\hskip 53.7072pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-53.7072pt\hskip 53.7072ptH_{B_{R}}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(s)-\overline{u}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-53.7072pt\hskip 53.7072pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-53.7072pt\hskip 53.7072ptH_{B_{R}}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(s)-\overline{u}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-42.39719pt\hskip 42.39719pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-42.39719pt\hskip 42.39719ptH_{B_{R}}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|{\overline{u}}_{n}(s)-\overline{u}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-39.38342pt\hskip 39.38342pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-39.38342pt\hskip 39.38342ptH_{B_{R}}}}}\,\,\mathrm{d}s.\biggr)

Thus the claim (
5.53
) follows from claims (
4.7
) and (
4.10
). Because the space
\mathscr{V}
is dense in
V_{s}
, we can easily deduce that (
5.53
) holds for all
\psi,\phi\in U
. The details are omitted. An interested reader can compare with the last part of the proof of
[
17
, Lemma B.1]
.
Hence the proof of claim (
5.46
) is complete.
∎

All this completes the proof of the assertion (iii) and also the Lemma
5.14
.
∎

We are now ready to present the proof of Lemma
5.11
.

Proof of Lemma
5.11
.

The proof is very similar to the proof of Lemma
5.10
, thus we will only outline the main differences of the two proofs.

Step 1.
The aim in this step is to prove that for each
t\in[0,T]
the random variable
\overline{N}_{\phi}(t)
is square integrable. In order to do this we let
C
be the set defined in (
5.32
) and we fix
s,t\in C
, with
s\leq t
, and
\psi\in U
.

With Lemma
5.14
at hand, we can argue as in the proof of Lemma
5.10
and prove that

\lim_{n\to\infty}({\overline{N}}_{n,\phi}(t)-{\overline{N}}_{n,\phi}(s))={\overline{N}_{\phi}(t)-\overline{N}_{\phi}(s)},\quad\overline{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{ - a.s.}

From the equality of laws of
u_{n}
and
\overline{u}_{n}
, the fact that both
N_{n,\phi}
and
{\overline{N}}_{n,\phi}
are square integrable martingales,
the embedding
U\hookrightarrow{H}
, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
and the application of the BDG inequality we infer that there exists a constant
c_{1}>0
independent of
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\overline{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\lvert{\overline{N}}_{n,\phi}(t)\lvert^{2}\bigr]=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\lvert N_{n,\phi}(t)\rvert^{2}\bigr]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lvert N_{n,\phi}(t)\rvert^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{U}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{Y}\rvert P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)|^{2}_{H}\lvert u_{n}(s)\rvert^{2}_{H}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr].

Since the restriction to
{H}
of the map
{P}_{n}
is the orthogonal projection from
{H}
onto
{H}_{n}
,
by the linear growth condition (
3.5
) in Assumption F.
2
, we see that there exists a constant
c_{2}>0
independent of
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\overline{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\lvert{\overline{N}}_{n,\phi}(t)\lvert^{2}\bigr]\leq c_{2}\lVert\psi\rVert_{U}^{2}\left(1+\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\lvert u_{n}(s)\rvert^{4}\right]\right).

(5.54)

From the last inequality (
5.54
) along with the estimate (
4.3
)
with
p=4
, we deduce that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\overline{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\lvert{\overline{N}}_{n,\phi}(t)\lvert^{2}\bigr]<\infty.

Owing to the convergence (
5.57
), the Fatou Lemma and (
5
) we can argue as in the proof of (
5.35
) and show that

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in C}\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\lvert{\overline{N}_{\phi}(r)}\rvert^{2}\right]<\infty,

(5.55)

which yields the square integrability of
\overline{N}_{\phi}(t)
for each
t\in C
.

For general
t\in[0,T]
we consider a
C
-valued sequence
(t_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
t_{m}\searrow t
as
m\to\infty
.
Note that (
5.55
) implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\lvert{\overline{N}_{\phi}(t_{m})}\rvert^{2}\right]<\infty.

(5.56)

Observe also that
\overline{N}_{\phi}^{2}(t_{m})
satisfies (
5.56
). Owing to the right-continuity of the paths of
\overline{N}_{\phi}
we have

\overline{N}^{2}_{\phi}(t_{m})\to\overline{N}^{2}_{\phi}(t),\mbox{ $\overline{\mathbb{P}}$-a.s.}

This along with the Fatou Lemma and (
5.56
) imply that
\overline{N}_{\phi}(t)
is square integrable for each
t\in[0,T]
.
This completes the first step of the proof.

Step 2.
Following the idea of Lemma
5.10
we denote by
\mathscr{D}_{t}^{0}
the
\sigma
-algebra generated by all maps

\mathbb{D}([0,T],U^{\prime})\ni x\mapsto x(s)\in U^{\prime},\,s\leq t.

We also set
\mathscr{D}_{t}=\bigcap_{s>t}\mathscr{D}^{0}_{s},\,t\in[0,T]
. Note that
\mathbf{D}=(\mathscr{D}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
is a filtration.
Let
C
be the set from the assertion (a) of Lemma
5.12
. Let us choose and fix
s,t\in C
, with
s\leq t
, and
\psi\in U
. Let us note that the almost sure set considered below is independent of
\psi
. Let

h\colon\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})\to\mathbb{R}

be a bounded, continuous function and
\mathscr{D}_{s}
-measurable.

With Lemma
5.14
at hand, we can argue as in the proof of Lemma
5.10
and prove that

\lim_{n\to\infty}h({\overline{u}}_{n})({\overline{N}}_{n,\phi}(t)-{\overline{N}}_{n,\phi}(s))=h(\overline{u})({\overline{N}_{\phi}(t)-\overline{N}_{\phi}(s)}),\quad\overline{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{ - a.s.}

(5.57)

Let us denote

{g}_{n}(\omega)\coloneqq\bigl(\overline{N}_{n,\phi}(t,\omega)-{\overline{N}}_{n,\phi}(s,\omega)\bigr)\,h\bigl({\overline{u}}_{n}\bigr),\qquad\omega\in\overline{\Omega}.

Thanks to (
5.56
) we can use the same idea as in the proof of assertion (
5.39
) and prove that

\sup_{n\geq 1}\overline{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[{|{g}_{n}|}^{2}\bigr]<\infty,

which yields the uniform integrability of the sequence
\{{g}_{n}{\}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Now, the convergence
(
5.57
), the uniform integrability of
\{{g}_{n}{\}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, the martingale property of
\overline{N}_{n,\phi}(t)
and the Vitali Convergence Theorem yield that for all
s,t\in C
,
s\leq t

\lim_{n\to\infty}\overline{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\bigl({\overline{N}}_{n,\phi}(t)-{\overline{N}}_{n,\phi}(s)\bigr)h\bigl({\overline{u}}_{n}\bigr)\bigr]=\overline{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\bigl(\overline{N}_{\phi}(t)-\overline{N}_{\phi}(s)\bigr)h\bigl(\overline{u}\bigr)\bigr]=0.

Hence, for
s,t\in C
with
s\leq t
we have

\overline{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\bigl(\overline{N}_{\phi}(t)-\overline{N}_{\phi}(s)\bigr)h\bigl(\overline{u}\bigr)\bigr]=0.

For general
t,s\in[0,T]
with
s<t
we consider two
C
-valued sequences
(t_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}},(s_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfying

•

s<s_{m}
and
t<t_{m}
for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

•

s_{m}\leq t_{m}
for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

•

s_{m}\searrow s
and
t_{m}\searrow t
as
m\to\infty
.

By the right-continuity of
\overline{N}_{\phi}
we see that
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.

\overline{N}_{\phi}(t_{m})\to\overline{N}_{\phi}(t)\mbox{ and }\overline{N}_{\phi}(s_{m})\to\overline{N}_{\phi}(s).

Notice also that, as above, we can use (
5.56
) and show that
the sequences
h(\overline{u})\overline{N}_{\phi}(t_{m})
and
h(\overline{u})\overline{N}_{\phi}(s_{m})
are uniformly integrable. Thus, we can proceed as in Step 2 of the proof of Lemma
5.10
and prove that

\lim_{m\to\infty}\overline{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\bigl(\overline{N}_{\phi}(t_{m})-\overline{N}_{\phi}(s_{m})\bigr)h\bigl(\overline{u}\bigr)\bigr]=\overline{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\bigl(\overline{N}_{\phi}(t)-\overline{N}_{\phi}(s)\bigr)h\bigl(\overline{u}\bigr)\bigr]=0.

This also completes the proof of the Lemma
5.11
.

∎

6
Existence of solutions

In this section we use the Assumptions F.
2
and F.
5
. When we use the latter assumption, we specify this explicitly.
In particular, we assume these assumptions in our Lemma
6.15
. To be precise we assume the growth condition (
3.7
) in Assumption F.
5
with
p=4
only and then use, also with
p=4
, the a’priori estimates (
4.3
) from Lemma
4.2
.

Hereafter, without loss of generality, we can and will assume that the external force
f
in equation (
3.1
) is equal to
0
.

6.1
Preliminaries

In this subsection we will discuss a notion of a purely discontinuous martingale.

Let us begin by recalling the following standard, well known fact, see

[
35
, §I.4e and Theorem I.4.2]
.

Proposition 6.1
.

If
M
and
N
are two locally square integrable
\mathbb{R}
-valued martingales, then there exists a unique (up to an evanescent set) predictable
\mathbb{R}
-valued process of finite variation
\langle M,N\rangle
such that the process

MN-\langle M,N\rangle

is a local martingale.

Definition 6.2
.

If
M
and
N
are two locally square integrable
\mathbb{R}
-valued martingales, then the process

\langle M,N\rangle

from Proposition
6.1
is called
the
predictable quadratic covariation process
of (the processes)
M
and
N
.

Note that while Proposition
6.1
and Definition
6.2
are stated for real-valed martingales, they have natural extension to Hilbert space valued martingales, see below and
[
45
, Section 20]
.

The following definition presented directly in an infinite dimensional version is taken from

[
45
, Theorem 20.5]
.

Definition 6.3
.

If
M
is a square integrable martingale taking values in a Hilbert space
X
with norm
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|\cdot|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.33336pt\hskip 8.33336pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.33336pt\hskip 8.33336pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|\cdot|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.33336pt\hskip 8.33336pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.33336pt\hskip 8.33336pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|\cdot|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-7.125pt\hskip 7.125pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-7.125pt\hskip 7.125pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|\cdot|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-6.87506pt\hskip 6.87506pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-6.87506pt\hskip 6.87506pt}}}
, then
the quadratic variation process of
M
is an
\mathbb{R}
-valued process
[M,M]
such that for every
t\in[0,T]
,
[M,M](t)
is the limit in
L^{1}=L^{1}(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P})
over the partitions of
[0,T]
with mesh converging to
0
, i.e.

[M,M](t)=L^{1}-\lim\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|M(t_{i}^{n}\wedge t)-M(t_{i-1}^{n}\wedge t)\right|^{2}.

(6.1)

The existence and further properties of the processes
[M,M]
was proven in
[
45
, Theorem 20.5]
.

According to
[
35
, Theorem 4.47]
, whose proof can be traced back to
[
35
, Proposition 4.44 and Theorem 4.31]
, one can show
the following variation of (
6.1
), namely that the convergence is in probability, uniformly on compact intervals, i.e. for every
T>0
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(M(t_{i}^{n}\wedge t)-M(t_{i-1}^{n}\wedge t)\right)^{2}-[M,M]_{t}\bigg|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\mathbb{P}}}}{{\to}}0.

(6.2)

For two square integrable
\mathbb{R}
-valued martingales
M
and
N
, the bracket
[M,N]
is defined by the polarisation formula, i.e.

[M,N]\coloneqq\frac{1}{4}[M+N,M+N]-\frac{1}{4}[M-N,M-N].

(6.3)

The following result, see
[
35
, Theorem I.4.52]
,
[
39
, Theorem 3.4.2]
and
[
45
, Theorem 20.5]
, relates these two brackets. We will use notation (
1.2
).

Proposition 6.4
.

If
M
and
N
are two square integrable martingales taking values in Hilbert space
(X,\langle.,.\rangle_{X})
, then

[M,N]_{t}=\langle M^{\mathrm{c}},N^{\mathrm{c}}\rangle_{t}+\sum_{s\in[0,t]}\langle\Delta M(s),\Delta N(s)\rangle_{X},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{-a.s., for every }t\in[0,T].

(6.4)

For the following definition, see
[
39
, Definition 3.4.1]
or
[
35
, Definition I.4.11]
, and
[
43
]
or
[
45
, Theorem 20.5]
for the infinite dimensional case.

Definition 6.5
.

A square integrable
\mathbb{R}
-valued (local) martingale
M
such that
M(0)=0
is purely discontinuous if and only if for any square integrable real continuous martingale
N
the product
MN
is a (local) martingale.

The following result is taken from
[
35
, Theorem I.4.18]
.

Proposition 6.6
.

Every
\mathbb{R}
-valued local martingale
M
can be uniquely, up to indistinguishability, represented as a sum of a continuous local martingale
M^{c}
and purely discontinuous local martingale
M^{d}
, i.e.

M=M^{c}+M^{d}.

The continuous part
X^{c}
of a semimartingale
X
is defined as the continuous part of the martingale part of
X
, see
[
35
, Proposition I.4.27]
.

As a consequence of Proposition
6.4
, one deduces the following result, see
[
39
, Th. 3.4.2]
.

Corollary 6.7
.

A square integrable
\mathbb{R}
-valued martingale
M
is purely discontinuous if and only if for every
t\in[0,T]
,

[M,M]_{t}=\sum_{s\leq t}|\Delta M(s)|^{2},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{-a.s.}.

(6.5)

The assertion of the Corollary above is often used as a definition of a purely discontinuous martingale, see e.g.
[
66
, page 156]
.
For vector-valued martingales we follow the recent papers
[
65
, Section 6]
or
[
66
, p. 156]
by Yaroslavtsev.

Definition 6.8
.

Let
(X,\langle.,.\rangle_{X})
be a Hilbert space.
A X-valued martingale
M
is purely discontinuous if for every
h\in{X}
the
\mathbb{R}
-valued martingale
\langle M,h\rangle_{X}
is purely discontinuous.

To define the predictable quadratic covariations of Hilbert space valued martingales we follow
[
45
, Theorem 18.6]
.

For two square integrable martingales
M
and
N
taking values in a Hilbert space
X
the quadratic covariations are defined as in
[
45
, Theorem 23.4]
.

Definition 6.9
.

If
M
and
N
are two square integrable martingales taking values in a Hilbert space
(X,\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{X})
, then
the predictable quadratic covariation
\langle M,N\rangle
of processes
M
and
N
is the unique predictable real process of finite variation such that the process

\langle M,N\rangle_{X}-\langle M,N\rangle

is a martingale.

6.2
Identification of the quadratic variation

In this, as well in all subsequent subsections, we assume that
T>0
is given and fixed and we consider

•

the probability space
\bigl(\overline{\Omega},\overline{\mathscr{F}},\overline{\mathbb{P}}\bigr)
introduced at the beginning of subsection
4.3
, see formula (
4.4
);

•

\mathscr{Z}_{T}
-valued random variables
\overline{u}
defined on
\bigl(\overline{\Omega},\overline{\mathscr{F}},\overline{\mathbb{P}}\bigr)
such that (
4.5
) holds;

•

the filtration
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}\coloneqq({\overline{\mathscr{F}}}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
, defined in equality (
5.26
);

•

the process
\overline{M}
defined in formula (
5.27
).

Let us recall that according to Remark
4.6

the
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
-valued random variables
\overline{u}
defined on
\bigl(\overline{\Omega},\overline{\mathscr{F}},\overline{\mathbb{P}}\bigr)
induces
a measurable process (see Remark
4.6
)

\overline{u}\colon[0,T]\times\overline{\Omega}\to V\cap{H}_{\mathrm{loc}},

whose trajectories belong to the space

\mathscr{Z}_{T}
.

Let us also choose and fix an arbitrary element
\phi\in U

and let us define a
\mathbb{R}
-valued process

\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
by

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{M}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.56949pt\hskip 34.56949pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-34.56949ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.56949ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{M}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.56949pt\hskip 34.56949pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-34.56949ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.56949ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{M}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.12093pt\hskip 29.12093pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-29.12093ptU^{\prime}\hskip 29.12093ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{M}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.23514pt\hskip 27.23514pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-27.23514ptU^{\prime}\hskip 27.23514ptU}}}

(6.6)

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.56949pt\hskip 34.56949pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-34.56949ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.56949ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.56949pt\hskip 34.56949pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-34.56949ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.56949ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.12093pt\hskip 29.12093pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-29.12093ptU^{\prime}\hskip 29.12093ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.23514pt\hskip 27.23514pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-27.23514ptU^{\prime}\hskip 27.23514ptU}}}-\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\bar{u}(0),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.95839pt\hskip 35.95839pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-35.95839ptU^{\prime}\hskip 35.95839ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\bar{u}(0),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.95839pt\hskip 35.95839pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-35.95839ptU^{\prime}\hskip 35.95839ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\bar{u}(0),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.08621pt\hskip 30.08621pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-30.08621ptU^{\prime}\hskip 30.08621ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\bar{u}(0),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.81966pt\hskip 27.81966pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-27.81966ptU^{\prime}\hskip 27.81966ptU}}}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\mathscr{A}\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.14589pt\hskip 43.14589pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-43.14589ptU^{\prime}\hskip 43.14589ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\mathscr{A}\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.14589pt\hskip 43.14589pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-43.14589ptU^{\prime}\hskip 43.14589ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\mathscr{A}\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.77722pt\hskip 35.77722pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-35.77722ptU^{\prime}\hskip 35.77722ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\mathscr{A}\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.67041pt\hskip 32.67041pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-32.67041ptU^{\prime}\hskip 32.67041ptU}}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle B(\overline{u}(s)),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-51.51048pt\hskip 51.51048pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-51.51048ptU^{\prime}\hskip 51.51048ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle B(\overline{u}(s)),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-51.51048pt\hskip 51.51048pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-51.51048ptU^{\prime}\hskip 51.51048ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle B(\overline{u}(s)),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-42.50743pt\hskip 42.50743pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-42.50743ptU^{\prime}\hskip 42.50743ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle B(\overline{u}(s)),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-38.60272pt\hskip 38.60272pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-38.60272ptU^{\prime}\hskip 38.60272ptU}}}\,\mathrm{d}s,\;\;t\in[0,T],

where
\overline{M}
is the process defined in formula (
5.27
).

Let us immediately observe that in view of Lemma
5.10

the process
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
is a square integrable
\mathbb{R}
-valued
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}
-martingale and
all its trajectories belong to
\mathbb{D}([0,T])
.

We begin with the analysis of the first process appearing on the right-hand side of the above identity (
6.6
).

Lemma 6.10
.

The process
\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 23.18057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.18057ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.18057ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 23.18057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.18057ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.18057ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.85005pt\hskip 19.85005pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-19.85005ptU^{\prime}\hskip 19.85005ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.0001pt\hskip 19.0001pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-19.0001ptU^{\prime}\hskip 19.0001ptU}}}\coloneqq\bigl\{\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.56949pt\hskip 34.56949pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-34.56949ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.56949ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.56949pt\hskip 34.56949pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-34.56949ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.56949ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.12093pt\hskip 29.12093pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-29.12093ptU^{\prime}\hskip 29.12093ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.23514pt\hskip 27.23514pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-27.23514ptU^{\prime}\hskip 27.23514ptU}}}:\,t\in[0,T]\bigr\}

is a
\mathbb{R}
-valued semimartingale.
Moreover, its quadratic variation process is equal to the quadratic variation process of the process
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
, i.e.
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.

[\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 23.18057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.18057ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.18057ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 23.18057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.18057ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.18057ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.85005pt\hskip 19.85005pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-19.85005ptU^{\prime}\hskip 19.85005ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.0001pt\hskip 19.0001pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-19.0001ptU^{\prime}\hskip 19.0001ptU}}},\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 23.18057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.18057ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.18057ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.0001pt\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}},\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}](t),\;\;t\in[0,T].

(6.7)

Proof of Lemma
6.10
.

Let us begin by an observation that according to formula (
5.27
) and Lemma
5.10
, it follows from definition of a semimartingale,
[
35
, Definition I.4.21]
, that the process
\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 23.18057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.18057ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.18057ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 23.18057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.18057ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.18057ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.85005pt\hskip 19.85005pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-19.85005ptU^{\prime}\hskip 19.85005ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.0001pt\hskip 19.0001pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-19.0001ptU^{\prime}\hskip 19.0001ptU}}}
is a real semimartingale.
Moreover,
because its finite variation part is a continuous process, identity (
6.7
) follow immediately from
[
35
, Proposition I.4.49(d)]
.

The proof of Lemma
6.10
is complete.
∎

Proposition 6.11
.

The predictable quadratic variation of the martingale
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
satisfies
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.

\langle\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}\rangle(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,\overline{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s,\;\;t\in[0,T].

(6.8)

Proof of Proposition
6.11
.

We follow the lines of the proof of
[
39
, Lemma 6.1.10]
.
Let us choose and fix an arbitrary element
\phi\in U
. Let us note that the process defined by the right hand side of equality (
6.8
) is continuous and therefore is
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}
-predictable. Thus it is enough to show that the difference

(\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}})^{2}(t)-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,\overline{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s.\;\;t\in[0,T],

is a martingale.

Step 1.

Applying the Itô formula (
A.2
) to the
\mathbb{R}
-valued semimartingale
\xi=\bigl\{\xi(t)=\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.56949pt\hskip 34.56949pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-34.56949ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.56949ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.56949pt\hskip 34.56949pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-34.56949ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.56949ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.12093pt\hskip 29.12093pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-29.12093ptU^{\prime}\hskip 29.12093ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.23514pt\hskip 27.23514pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-27.23514ptU^{\prime}\hskip 27.23514ptU}}},\;t\in[0,T]\bigr\}
, see (

(6.9)

-valued process

, see formula (

), we find that the above equality (

) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \overline{N}_{\phi}(t)&=2\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s-),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip &\qquad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,\overline{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s,\;\;\;\;t\in[0,T].\end{aligned}

From Lemma
6.10

we infer that
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \overline{N}_{\phi}(t)&=2\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s-),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip &\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,\overline{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s,\;\;\;\;t\in[0,T].\end{aligned}

The last equality can be rearranged as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}(t))^{2}&-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,\overline{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\bigl((\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip
