0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}(t))^{2}-[\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}},\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}](t)\bigr)+\overline{N}_{\phi}(t)-2\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s-),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.20137pt\hskip 41.20137pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.20137ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.20137ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s-),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.20137pt\hskip 41.20137pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.20137ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.20137ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s-),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.56886pt\hskip 32.56886pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-32.56886ptU^{\prime}\hskip 32.56886ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s-),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.1287pt\hskip 30.1287pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-30.1287ptU^{\prime}\hskip 30.1287ptU}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}(s),\;\;\;\;t\in[0,T].\end{aligned}

(6.10)

Step 2.
Let us note that each of the three processes on the right hand side of equality (
6.10
) is a martingale. Indeed,
\overline{N}_{\phi}
is a martingale by Lemma
5.11
, the process expressed as integral is a local martingale by
[
35
, Theorem I.4.40a]

and the process
(\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}})^{2}-[\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}},\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}]
is a martingale by
[
45
, Corollary 2 p. 125]
.

Thus the proof of Proposition
6.11
is complete.
∎

6.3
Pure discontinuity of the martingale
M_{\phi}

In a forthcoming Proposition
6.12
we will prove that for every
\phi\in U
,
the martingale
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
defined in (
6.6
), is purely discontinuous. This result is an analogue of
[
39
, Theorem 6.1.3]
.

We begin with formulating the main result of this subsection. After this has been achieved we will present three lemmata which will be used in the proof of our main result. Let us emphasize that these two results are in the framework of the original probability space while the main result is in the framework of the new probability space. At the end of this section we will turn back to the proof of the main result.

Proposition 6.12
.

Assume that
\phi\in U
.
The martingale
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
, defined in formula (
6.6
), is purely discontinuous.

Condition 6.13
.

Assume that we are given a function
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
that is Lipschitz and even function such that for some
a>0

\displaystyle 0\leq g(r)\leq r^{2}\;\;\mbox{ for }r\in[-a,a].

(6.11)

Note that under Condition
6.13
, the function
g
satisfies the following linear growth condition

\displaystyle g(r)

\displaystyle\leq c|r|,\;\;r\in\mathbb{R},

(6.12)

as this is used in one of the proofs.

Lemma 6.14
.

Assume that a function
g\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfies Condition
6.13
.
If
{z}_{k}\to{z}
in
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
, then

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g({\bigl(P_{k}F(s,z_{k}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H})\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g({\bigl(F(s,z(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H})\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Proof.

The proof is based on the proof of
[
39
, Lemma 6.1.7]
.

Let us fix an arbitrary time instance
t\in[0,T]
and a
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
-valued sequence
({z}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
{z}_{k}\to{z}
in
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
.
We will show that

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(P_{k}F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y)-F(s,{z}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\to 0.

(6.13)

We have

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(P_{k}F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y)-F(s,{z}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(P_{k}F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y)-F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y)-F(s,{z}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s.

(6.14)

Since by assumptions
{z}_{k}\to{z}
in the space
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
,
by Assumption F.
3
we infer that

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y)-F(s,{z}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\to 0.

(6.15)

Secondly, by the Vitali Theorem, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(P_{k}F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y)-F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

(6.16)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y),(P_{k}-\Id)\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\to 0.

Let us note that the Vitali Convergence Theorem can be applied here because by inequality (
3.7
)
and assertion (
4.10
) we have

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y),(P_{k}-\Id)\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{4}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\leq 2\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825pt4}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825pt4}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.55833pt\hskip 9.55833pt4}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.55833pt\hskip 9.55833ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.79175pt\hskip 8.79175pt4}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.79175pt\hskip 8.79175ptH}}}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-65.24423pt\hskip 65.24423pt4}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-65.24423pt\hskip 65.24423ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-65.24423pt\hskip 65.24423pt4}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-65.24423pt\hskip 65.24423ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-48.26045pt\hskip 48.26045pt4}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-48.26045pt\hskip 48.26045ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.18326pt\hskip 43.18326pt4}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.18326pt\hskip 43.18326ptH}}}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{4}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825pt4}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825pt4}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.55833pt\hskip 9.55833pt4}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.55833pt\hskip 9.55833ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.79175pt\hskip 8.79175pt4}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.79175pt\hskip 8.79175ptH}}}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|{z}_{k}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.25351pt\hskip 30.25351pt4}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.25351pt\hskip 30.25351ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|{z}_{k}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.25351pt\hskip 30.25351pt4}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.25351pt\hskip 30.25351ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|{z}_{k}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.2536pt\hskip 23.2536pt4}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.2536pt\hskip 23.2536ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|{z}_{k}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-21.35086pt\hskip 21.35086pt4}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-21.35086pt\hskip 21.35086ptH}}})\,\mathrm{d}s<\infty.

(6.17)

Now, consider an arbitrary subsequence
(n_{k})
.
Since, by (
6.13
),

{({P}_{n_{k}}F(s,{z}_{n_{k}}(s);y),\phi)}_{H}\to{(F(s,z(s);y),\phi)}_{H}\mbox{ in }L^{2}([0,t]\times Y;\Leb\otimes\nu),

we can choose a further subsequence, which for simplicity of notation is denoted by
(k)
,
such that

{(P_{k}F(s,z_{k}(s);y),\phi)}_{H}\;\to\;{(F(s,z(s);y),\phi)}_{H}\;\;\;\Leb\otimes\nu\mbox{-almost everywhere}.

By the continuity of the function
g
we infer that

g({\bigl(F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H})\to g({\bigl(F(s,z(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}),\;\;\;\Leb\otimes\nu\mbox{-almost everywhere}.

Note that (
6.13
) implies the convergence of the
L^{2}
-norms:

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(P_{k}F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,{z}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Moreover, since
\Leb\otimes\nu
-almost everywhere

g\left({\bigl(P_{k}F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\right)\leq c{\bigl(P_{k}F(s,{z}_{k}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2},

and
{z}
belongs to
L^{2}(0,T;V)
we infer that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,{z}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s&\leq C_{2}\left\lvert\phi\right\rvert_{H}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}(1+\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|z(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.55551pt\hskip 27.55551pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.55551pt\hskip 27.55551ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|z(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.55551pt\hskip 27.55551pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.55551pt\hskip 27.55551ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|z(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-18.88203pt\hskip 18.88203pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-18.88203pt\hskip 18.88203ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|z(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.9793pt\hskip 16.9793pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.9793pt\hskip 16.9793ptH}}})\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq C_{2}\left\lvert\phi\right\rvert_{H}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}(1+\left\lVert z(s)\right\rVert_{V}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}s<\infty.\end{aligned}

Thus, by
[
6
, Theorem 2.8.8]
we infer that

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g({\bigl(P_{k}F(s,z_{k}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H})\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g({\bigl(F(s,z(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H})\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Since this holds for a subsequence of an arbitrary subsequence, it follows that this convergence holds for the original sequence.
This finishes the proof of the claimed assertion. Therefore, the proof of Lemma
6.14
is complete.
∎

Let us recall that in the following result
u_{n}
is the unique solution to (
4.1
). In particular,
u_{n}
is an
U^{\prime}
-valued càdlàg process.

Lemma 6.15
.

Assume that
\phi\in U
and a function
g\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}

satisfies Condition
6.13
.
Then, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
t\in[0,T]
, the following identities hold
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely,

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g(\Delta\langle{u}_{n}(s),\phi\rangle)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g\bigl({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s-),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\,\bigr){\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y),

(6.18)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g\bigl({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s-),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\,\bigr)\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g(\Delta\langle{u}_{n}(s),\phi\rangle)

(6.19)

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g\bigl({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\,\bigr)\mathrm{d}s\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y).

Proof of Lemma
6.15
.

Let us choose and fix an arbitrary
t\in[0,T]
.
Let us begin with an observation that the integrals in (
6.18
) and (
6.19
) are well defined and almost surely finite. This is true provided the integrand belongs to
\mathbf{F}_{p}^{1}\cap\mathbf{F}_{p}^{2}
, see
[
15
, Section 3]
.
Below we will show that this is the case. For this purpose, we first apply, (
6.12
), the linear growth condition (
3.5
) in Assumption F.
2
and the a’priori estimates (
4.3
) with
p=2
from Lemma
4.2
to infer the following chain of inequalities.

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}|g\bigl({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\,\bigr)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\right]

(6.20)

\displaystyle\leq c^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}|{\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}|^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle=c^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,{u}_{n}(s),y),P_{n}\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle\leq c^{2}|P_{n}\phi|_{H}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}|F(s,{u}_{n}(s),y)|^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle\leq c^{2}|\phi|_{H}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}|F(s,{u}_{n}(s),y)|^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle\leq c^{2}C_{2}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.55833pt\hskip 9.55833pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.55833pt\hskip 9.55833ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.79175pt\hskip 8.79175pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.79175pt\hskip 8.79175ptH}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}(1+\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.4064pt\hskip 24.4064pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.4064pt\hskip 24.4064ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.4852pt\hskip 22.4852pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.4852pt\hskip 22.4852ptH}}})\,\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle\leq c^{2}C_{2}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-15.95825pt\hskip 15.95825ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.55833pt\hskip 9.55833pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.55833pt\hskip 9.55833ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|\phi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.79175pt\hskip 8.79175pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.79175pt\hskip 8.79175ptH}}}\Bigl(T+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.41095pt\hskip 31.41095ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.4064pt\hskip 24.4064pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.4064pt\hskip 24.4064ptH}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|{u}_{n}(s)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.4852pt\hskip 22.4852pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.4852pt\hskip 22.4852ptH}}})\,\mathrm{d}s\right]\Bigr)<\infty.

Secondly, since
g
satisfies condition (
6.11
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle\int_{Y}|g\bigl({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\,\bigr)|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq c{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}|{\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}|^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg]<\infty.

Let
\phi\in U
and
g\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be as in the statement of the Lemma. We will prove (
6.18
).
It follows from (
4.1
) that
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\Delta\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.81377pt\hskip 41.81377pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.81377ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.81377ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.81377pt\hskip 41.81377pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.81377ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.81377ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.50644pt\hskip 34.50644pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-34.50644ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.50644ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.90192pt\hskip 31.90192pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-31.90192ptU^{\prime}\hskip 31.90192ptU}}}\;=\;\Delta{(u_{n}(s),\phi)}_{H}

\displaystyle=\;\Delta\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y).

Since the integrand belongs to
{\mathbf{F}}_{p}^{1}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}r)

\displaystyle=\;\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\eta(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y)-\underset{=\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r),y,\phi)}_{H}\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\mathrm{d}r}{\underbrace{\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\mathrm{d}r}}.

Since moreover,

\Delta\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r),y,\phi)}_{H}\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\mathrm{d}r\;=\;0,

we have

\Delta\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y)\;=\;\Delta\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\eta(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y).

Thus

\displaystyle\Delta\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.81377pt\hskip 41.81377pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.81377ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.81377ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.81377pt\hskip 41.81377pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.81377ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.81377ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.50644pt\hskip 34.50644pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-34.50644ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.50644ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.90192pt\hskip 31.90192pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-31.90192ptU^{\prime}\hskip 31.90192ptU}}}

\displaystyle=\;\Delta\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\eta(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y).

Note that the processes
{(\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y))}_{s\in[0,T]}
and

{(\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\eta(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}r))}_{s\in[0,T]}
are càdlàg.
We have

\displaystyle\Delta\int_{0}^{s}\int_{Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\eta(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\;\iint_{(0,s]\times Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\eta(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y)-\lim_{m\to\infty}\iint_{(0,s_{m}]\times Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\eta(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y)

where
{(t_{m})}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,s)
is a sequence such that
s_{m}\nearrow s
.
We will use the definition of the integral with respect of the Poisson random measure.
Let
p
be the
\mathbb{F}
-optional
Y
-valued point process corresponding to the Poisson random measure
\eta
and let
{D}_{p}
denote its domain

\displaystyle\iint_{(0,s]\times Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\eta(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y)-\iint_{(0,s_{m}]\times Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\eta(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\;\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s_{m}<r\leq s}}{{r\in{D}_{p}}}}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),p(r),\phi)}_{H}{1\!\!1}_{{D}_{p}}(r)

Passing to the limit as
m\to\infty
we infer that

\displaystyle\iint_{(0,s]\times Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\eta(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\lim_{m\to\infty}\iint_{(0,s_{m}]\times Y}{(P_{n}F(r,u_{n}(r-),y,\phi)}_{H}\eta(\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\;{(P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),p(s),\phi)}_{H}{1\!\!1}_{{D}_{p}}(s).

Thus we get

\Delta\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.81377pt\hskip 41.81377pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.81377ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.81377ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-41.81377pt\hskip 41.81377pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-41.81377ptU^{\prime}\hskip 41.81377ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.50644pt\hskip 34.50644pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-34.50644ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.50644ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),\phi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.90192pt\hskip 31.90192pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-31.90192ptU^{\prime}\hskip 31.90192ptU}}}\;=\;{(P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),p(s),\phi)}_{H}{1\!\!1}_{{D}_{p}}(s).

Let us next observe that because
g(0)=0
we have, for every
(\omega,s)\in\Omega\times[0,T]
,

g\bigl({(P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),p(s),\phi)}_{H}{1\!\!1}_{{D}_{p}}(s)\bigr)\;=\;g\bigl({(P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),p(s),\phi)}_{H}\bigr){1\!\!1}_{{D}_{p}}(s)

Therefore, we deduce that for every
(\omega,t)\in\Omega\times[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g\bigl({(P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),p(s),\phi)}_{H}{1\!\!1}_{{D}_{p}}(s)\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\;\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g\bigl({(P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),p(s),\phi)}_{H}{1\!\!1}_{{D}_{p}}(s)\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\;\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g\bigl({(P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),p(s),\phi)}_{H}\bigr){1\!\!1}_{{D}_{p}}(s)

\displaystyle=\;\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g\bigl({(P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),y,\phi)}_{H}\bigr)\,\eta(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y).

Thus the proof of assertion (
6.18
) is complete.

Next let us observe that for each
n
, the definition of the integral with respect to a compensated Poisson random measure, see
[
39
, equality (3.4.1)]
and/or formula (
2.9
), we have
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g\bigl({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s-),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\,\bigr)\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g\bigl({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s-),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\,\bigr){\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g\bigl({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\,\bigr)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\mathrm{d}s.

By applying now equality (
6.18
) we deduce that equality (
6.19
) holds.
∎

The previous results were formulated on the original probability space.
The next result is formulated for the new filtered probability space as listed at the beginning of Subsection
6.2
.
Let us recall that
({\overline{u}}_{n})_{n}
is a sequence of
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
-valued random variables defined on the probability space
\bigl(\overline{\Omega},\overline{\mathscr{F}},\overline{\mathbb{P}}\bigr)
, see (
4.4
), such that

\displaystyle{\overline{u}}_{n}\to\overline{u}\mbox{ in }\mathscr{Z}_{T}\quad\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}.

The last assertion implies assertions (
4.6
)-(
4.9
).

For a topological space
X
, we define an evaluation map

\pi_{t}:\mathbb{D}([0,T];X)\ni z\mapsto z(t)\in X.

Lemma 6.16
.

Let us assume that a function
g\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}

satisfies Condition
6.13
.
Assume that
\phi\in U
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}
let us consider the maps

\Psi_{n},\Psi:\mathscr{Z}_{T}\to\mathbb{D}([0,T];\mathbb{R})

defined by for
u\in\mathscr{Z}_{T}
and
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\Psi_{n}(u)(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g(\Delta\langle u(s),\phi\rangle)-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g\left(\langle P_{n}F(s,u(s),y),\phi\rangle\right)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s,

(6.21)

\displaystyle\Psi(u)(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g(\Delta\langle u(s),\phi\rangle)-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g\left(\langle F(s,u(s),y),\phi\rangle\right)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s.

(6.22)

Then, for every
t\in[0,T]
, the maps
\pi_{t}\circ\Psi_{n}:\mathscr{Z}_{T}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\pi_{t}\circ\Psi:\mathscr{Z}_{T}\to\mathbb{R}
are measurable and, if

t_{0}\in C
, where the set
C=C(\overline{u})
has been defined in (
5.32
), the following equality holds

\lim_{n\to\infty}\Psi_{n}({\overline{u}}_{n})(t_{0})=\Psi(\overline{u})(t_{0})\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}.

(6.23)

Proof.

Let us choose and fix: an element
\phi\in U
, a function
g\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Let the set
C
be as in the statement of the lemma, see also (
5.32
).
Let us first observe that by
[
39
, Corollary 2.4.2]
the map

G:\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})\to\mathbb{D}([0,T];\mathbb{R}),\qquad G(z)(t)=\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g(\Delta z(s)),\;t\in[0,T],

is continuous. Combining this with the continuity of

\mathbb{D}([0,T];U^{\prime})\ni z\mapsto\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle z,\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.27086pt\hskip 23.27086pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.27086ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.27086ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle z,\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.27086pt\hskip 23.27086pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.27086ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.27086ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle z,\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-18.95772pt\hskip 18.95772pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-18.95772ptU^{\prime}\hskip 18.95772ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle z,\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.62857pt\hskip 17.62857pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-17.62857ptU^{\prime}\hskip 17.62857ptU}}}\in\mathbb{D}([0,T];\mathbb{R})

and the measurability of the evaluation map
\pi_{t}
,
see
[
4
, Theorem 12.5(ii)]
establishes the measurability of

\mathscr{Z}_{T}\ni u\mapsto\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g(\Delta\langle u(s),\phi\rangle)\in\mathbb{R}

For the measurability of the maps
\pi_{t}\circ\Psi_{n}
and
\pi_{t}\circ\Psi
, for
t\in[0,T]
,
it is sufficient to consider the case of
\pi_{t}\circ\Psi
as the measurability of
\pi_{t}\circ\Psi_{n}
is obtained through the identity
{\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}={\bigl(F(s,{u}(s),y),P_{n}\phi\bigr)}_{H}
. To finish this part of the proof it is sufficient to prove that the map

\mathscr{Z}_{T}\ni u\mapsto\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g({\bigl(F(s,{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H})\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\in\mathbb{R}

is continuous.
This can be done by repeating the argument in Lemma
6.14
.

We show (
6.23
).
Choose an arbitrary element
t_{0}\in C
.
Since by (
4.9
)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle{\overline{u}}_{n},\phi\rangle=\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 23.18057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.18057ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.18057ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 23.18057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.18057ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.18057ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.85005pt\hskip 19.85005pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-19.85005ptU^{\prime}\hskip 19.85005ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.0001pt\hskip 19.0001pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-19.0001ptU^{\prime}\hskip 19.0001ptU}}}\mbox{ in }\mathbb{D}([0,T])\;\;{\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}},

it follows from
[
39
, Corollary 2.4.2]
that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{s\in[0,\cdot]}g(\Delta\langle{\overline{u}}_{n}(s),\phi\rangle)=\sum_{s\in[0,\cdot]}g(\Delta\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.64587pt\hskip 35.64587pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-35.64587ptU^{\prime}\hskip 35.64587ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.64587pt\hskip 35.64587pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-35.64587ptU^{\prime}\hskip 35.64587ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.8744pt\hskip 29.8744pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-29.8744ptU^{\prime}\hskip 29.8744ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.76755pt\hskip 27.76755pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-27.76755ptU^{\prime}\hskip 27.76755ptU}}})\;\;\mbox{in $\mathbb{D}([0,T])$}{\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}},

i.e. the following sequence of functions

[0,T]\ni t\mapsto\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g(\Delta\langle{\overline{u}}_{n}(s),\phi\rangle)

converges in
\mathbb{D}([0,T])
to a function
[0,T]\ni t\mapsto\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g(\Delta\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.64587pt\hskip 35.64587pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-35.64587ptU^{\prime}\hskip 35.64587ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.64587pt\hskip 35.64587pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-35.64587ptU^{\prime}\hskip 35.64587ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.8744pt\hskip 29.8744pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-29.8744ptU^{\prime}\hskip 29.8744ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.76755pt\hskip 27.76755pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-27.76755ptU^{\prime}\hskip 27.76755ptU}}})

\mbox{ on }\overline{\Omega}
.

Since
t_{0}\in C
we infer that the process

t\mapsto\sum_{s\leq t}g(\Delta\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.64587pt\hskip 35.64587pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-35.64587ptU^{\prime}\hskip 35.64587ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.64587pt\hskip 35.64587pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-35.64587ptU^{\prime}\hskip 35.64587ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.8744pt\hskip 29.8744pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-29.8744ptU^{\prime}\hskip 29.8744ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.76755pt\hskip 27.76755pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-27.76755ptU^{\prime}\hskip 27.76755ptU}}}),

with probability
1
has no jump at
t_{0}
.
By the continuous mapping theorem, see
[
60
, Theorem 2.3]
and
[
4
, Theorem 12.5(i)]
,

\sum_{s\in[0,t_{0}]}g(\Delta\langle{\overline{u}}_{n}(s),\phi\rangle)\to\sum_{s\in[0,t_{0}]}g(\Delta\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.64587pt\hskip 35.64587pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-35.64587ptU^{\prime}\hskip 35.64587ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.64587pt\hskip 35.64587pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-35.64587ptU^{\prime}\hskip 35.64587ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.8744pt\hskip 29.8744pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-29.8744ptU^{\prime}\hskip 29.8744ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.76755pt\hskip 27.76755pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-27.76755ptU^{\prime}\hskip 27.76755ptU}}})\qquad\overline{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{ -a.s.}

(6.24)

On the other hand, by Lemma
6.14
we infer that
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely,

\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\int_{Y}g({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{\overline{u}}_{n}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H})\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\to\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\int_{Y}g({\bigl(F(s,\overline{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\,)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s.

By combining (
6.21
) and (
6.22
) with (
6.24
) and the last equality we complete the proof of the Lemma.
∎

Lemma 6.17
.

Assume that
\phi\in U
and a function
g\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}

satisfies Condition
6.13
. Let
C=C(\overline{u})
be the set defined by equality (
5.32
).
Then for every
t\in C
,

\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g(\Delta\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\rangle)-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g\left({\bigl(F(s,\overline{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\right)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]=0.

(6.25)

Proof of Lemma
6.17
.

Let us choose and fix
\phi\in U
and, for the time being, an arbitrary
t\in[0,T]
. We will prove
equality (
6.25
).
For this purpose we consider the maps
\Psi_{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\Psi\colon\mathscr{Z}_{T}\to\mathbb{D}([0,T];\mathbb{R})
defined by
(
6.21
) and (
6.22
) respectively. Since
g
satisfies Condition
6.13
,
from Lemma
6.15
we infer that

\displaystyle\bigl(\Psi_{n}(u_{n})\bigr)(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s-),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\,))\,\tilde{{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}s),\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{-a.s}.

(6.26)

On the other hand, by Lemma
6.14
the maps
\pi_{t}\circ\Psi_{n}\colon\mathscr{Z}_{T}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\pi_{t}\circ\Psi\colon\mathscr{Z}_{T}\to\mathbb{R}
are measurable. Hence, owing to the equality of laws of
{\overline{u}}_{n}
and
u_{n}
and (
6.26
) we see that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{n}(u_{n})(t)\right]=\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Psi_{n}({\overline{u}}_{n})(t)\right]=0

and

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\Psi_{n}({\overline{u}}_{n})(t)|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[|\Psi_{n}(u_{n})(t)|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),,\bigr)}_{y}){\phi}{H}\,)\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{2}\right].

From the last equality along with the Itô isometry for
\mathbb{R}
-valued processes and the same argument as in (
6.20
) we infer that there exist a constant
C_{0}>0
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[(\Psi_{n}({\overline{u}}_{n})(t))^{2}\right]={\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}|g({\bigl(P_{n}F(s,{u}_{n}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H})\,|^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]\leq C_{0}.

From now on we assume that
t\in C
.
Thus, the family
\{\Psi_{n}({\overline{u}}_{n})(t):n\in\mathbb{N}\}
is uniformly integrable and therefore by the Vitali Theorem and assertion (
6.23
) in Lemma
6.16
we deduce that

\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\Psi_{n}({\overline{u}}_{n})(t)]\to\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Psi(\overline{u})(t)\right].

Therefore we infer that

\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Psi(\overline{u})(t)\right]=0.

Thus, we have proved equality (
6.25
) as claimed.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.12
.

Let us choose and fix
\phi\in U
. Let
C
be the set defined by equality (
5.32
).
We divide the proof into two steps.

Step 1.
We will prove that the martingale
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
satisfies equality (
6.5
) for every
t\in C
. For this purpose let us choose and fix
t\in C
.

Let us consider a function
g\colon\mathbb{R}\ni r\mapsto r^{2}\in[0,\infty)
.
Then we can find a sequence
(g_{m})_{m=1}^{\infty}
of functions such that each
g_{m}
satisfies Condition
6.13
with
a=m
and
g_{m}(r)\nearrow g(r)
for all
r\in\mathbb{R}
.
Since
t\in C
, we can apply Lemma
6.17
and see that the identity (
6.25
) is satisfied with
g
replaced by
g_{m}
. Thus, by the Monotone Convergence Theorem we deduce that

\lim_{m\to\infty}\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{s\in[0,t]}g_{m}(\Delta\langle\overline{u}(s),\phi\rangle)-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}g_{m}\left({\bigl(F(s,\overline{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}\right)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]=0.

That is, we have

\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{s\in[0,t]}(\Delta\;\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.56949pt\hskip 34.56949pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-34.56949ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.56949ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.56949pt\hskip 34.56949pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-34.56949ptU^{\prime}\hskip 34.56949ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.12093pt\hskip 29.12093pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-29.12093ptU^{\prime}\hskip 29.12093ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-27.23514pt\hskip 27.23514pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-27.23514ptU^{\prime}\hskip 27.23514ptU}}})^{2}\right]=\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,\overline{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right].

(6.27)

Note that finiteness of both terms above follows form Assumption F.
2
and Lemma
4.8
.

Next let
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
be the martingale defined in (
6.6
). It follows from the proof of
[
35
, Proposition I.4.50b)]
that for every
t\in[0,T]
,

\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\langle\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}},\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}\rangle(t)\right]=\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[[\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}},\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}](t)\right].

From this equality along with

\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s. for
s\in[0,T]
,

\Delta\left(\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 23.18057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.18057ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.18057ptU}}}{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.18057pt\hskip 23.18057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.90308pt\hskip-23.18057ptU^{\prime}\hskip 23.18057ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.85005pt\hskip 19.85005pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-19.85005ptU^{\prime}\hskip 19.85005ptU}}}{{\hskip 7.96948pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bigl\langle\overline{u},\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.0001pt\hskip 19.0001pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.96948pt\hskip-19.0001ptU^{\prime}\hskip 19.0001ptU}}}\right)(s)=\Delta\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}(s)\mbox{ and }\langle\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}},\mathchoice{{\hskip by

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{s\in[0,t]}(\Delta\,\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.90308pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\bigl\langle\overline{M}(s),\phi\bigr\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip (6.28)

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}{\bigl(F(s,\overline{u}(s),y),\phi\bigr)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]=\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\langle\mathchoice{{\hskip Let us recall that by

\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.,

[\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip t}\bigl(\Delta\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}},\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptstyle\hskip (6.29)

Thus the random variable defined by (
6.29
) is, on the one hand non-negative, because the right-hand side of (
6.29
) is non-negative, and on the other hand, by (
6.28
), has expectation equal to
0
. Therefore this random variable is almost surely equal to
0
, i.e.

\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s

[\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}},\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}](t)-\sum_{s\in[0,t]}\bigl(\Delta\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}(s)\bigr)^{2}=0,\;\;t\in C.

Therefore the proof in Step 1 is complete.

Step 2.
In Step 1 we proved that
the martingale
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
satisfies equality (
6.5
) for every
t\in C
. Now we will show that it satisfies equality (
6.5
) for every
t\in[0,T]
.

By applying
[
35
, Lemma 4.51 and Theorem 4.52]
, see also formula (
6.29
) above, we infer
that the predictable quadratic variation of the process
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
is equal to
0
for every
t\in C
.
Since the process
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
is a continuous martingale and since by
[
45
, Proposition 17.2]
the predictable quadratic variation of a continuous martingale is continuous, by the density of the set
C
in the interval
[0,T]
, we infer
the predictable quadratic variation of the process
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\mathrm{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
is equal to
0
for every
t\in[0,T]
.
Thus, by
[
35
, Lemma 4.51 and Theorem 4.52]
, see also formula (
6.29
) above, we conclude
that the martingale
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
satisfies equality
(
6.5
) for every
t\in[0,T]
.
This finishes the proof of Step 2.

From Step 1 and Step 2 we conclude that the square integrable martingale
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\overline{M}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 5.00002pt\phi}}}
is a purely discontinuous martingale.
∎

6.4
Identifying the compensator
M_{\phi}

In this subsection we work in the framework of the new filtered probability space as listed at the beginning of Section
6.2
.
The main result of this section, i.e. Proposition
6.20
, states that for any Borel set
A
, separated from
0
, the process
\overline{N}_{A}
defined in (
6.32
) is a martingale. The proof consists of several preparatory lemmas. Firstly, we define some continuous functions
a_{k}
approximating an indicator function
\mathds{1}_{A}
and prove certain properties of those. Then we use them to construct two families of martingales
\overline{N}_{a_{k},n}
and
\overline{N}_{a_{k},n}
approximating
\overline{N}_{A}
. We then show that
\overline{N}_{A}
is martingale for every set which is closed, bounded and separated from
0
. Finally, the martingale property follows for any Borel set separated from
0
.

Note that in this whole sub-section we consider the processes and their jumps without any duality pairing. Let us recall that
according to Lemma
5.10
the process
{\overline{M}}
is a square integrable
U^{\prime}
-valued
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}
-martingale.
Moreover, by the same lemma, all trajectories of the martingale
{\overline{M}}
are
U^{\prime}
-valued càdlàg. Note that the
U^{\prime}
-valued jump process
\Delta\overline{M}
is well-defined.
Finally, by Proposition
6.12
, the martingale
{\overline{M}}
is purely discontinuous. Thus it follows from
[
39
, Corollary 2.4.2]
that for every continuous
function
\tilde{g}\colon U^{\prime}\to\mathbb{R}
vanishing in a ball around the origin
0\in U^{\prime}
, the real process
G(\overline{M})
defined by

\displaystyle(G(\overline{M}))(t)\coloneqq\sum\limits_{0<s\leq t}\tilde{g}(\Delta\overline{M}(s)),\;\;\;t\in[0,T],

(6.30)

is càdlàg and all its trajectories belong to the Skorokhod space
\mathbb{D}([0,T])
.

The process
\Delta\overline{M}
can also be seen as an
{H}
-valued process provided the limit in formula (
1.2
) is understood in the weak topology of
H
.
Moreover, since by Lemma
5.10
the process
\overline{M}
is
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}
-adapted and
U^{\prime}
-valued càdlàg, we infer from
[
35
, Corollary I.1.25]
and the separability of
U^{\prime}
that both the processes
\left(\overline{M}(t-)\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
and
\Delta\overline{M}
(resp.
G(\overline{M})
) are
U^{\prime}
-valued (resp.
\mathbb{R}
-valued)
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}
-optional.

Remark 6.18
.

Because
{\overline{M}}
is a
U^{\prime}
-valued càdlàg process, according to an infinite-dimensional version of

[
35
, Proposition II.1.16, p. 69]
, there exists an
\overline{\mathbb{N}}
-valued random measure
\mu
on
[0,T]\times U^{\prime}
such that for every
\overline{\omega}\in\overline{\Omega}
,

\mu(\overline{\omega};(s,t]\times A)=\sum_{r\in(s,t]}\mathds{1}_{\{\Delta\,\overline{M}(\overline{\omega},s)\not=0\}}\mathds{1}_{A}(\Delta\overline{M}(\overline{\omega},s))

for all pairs
(s,t)\in[0,T]^{2}_{+}
and every set
A\in\mathscr{B}(U^{\prime})
.
Hence,

\sum\limits_{0<s\leq t}\mathds{1}_{A}(\Delta\overline{M}(\overline{\omega},s))=\mu(\overline{\omega};(0,t]\times A),\;\;\overline{\omega}\in\overline{\Omega}.

Let us note that the left-hand side in the identity above is the
first term on the right-hand side of the definition (
6.32
) below.

Note also that by
[
35
, Definition II.1.13, p. 68]
, the random measure
\mu
is
\overline{\mathbb{F}}
-optional and, by
[
35
, Definition II.1.4 and Prop. I.1.24]
, the left-continuous and
\overline{\mathbb{F}}
-adapted function

\mathds{1}_{\overline{\Omega}\times(s,t]\times A}
is
\overline{\mathbb{F}}
-optional as well.
Therefore, by
[
35
, Definition II.1.6, p. 66]
we deduce that
the process

\overline{\Omega}\times[0,T]\ni(\overline{\omega},t)\mapsto\sum\limits_{0<s\leq t}\mathds{1}_{A}(\Delta\overline{M}(\overline{\omega},s))\in\mathbb{R}

is
\overline{\mathbb{F}}
-optional. Hence by
[
35
, Proposition I.1.21 a)]
, we infer that it is
\overline{\mathbb{F}}
-adapted.
In particular, for every
t\in[0,T]
, the random variable

\overline{\Omega}\ni\overline{\omega}\mapsto\sum\limits_{0<s\leq t}\mathds{1}_{A}(\Delta\overline{M}(s))\in\mathbb{R}

is
\bar{\mathscr{F}}_{t}
-measurable.

Definition 6.19
.

(i)

We denote by
\mathscr{A}_{0}
the set of Borel sets
A\subset U^{\prime}\setminus\{0\}
such that

\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum\limits_{0<s\leq T}\mathds{1}_{A}(\Delta\overline{M}(s))\right]<\infty.

(6.31)

(ii)

Let
(\alpha,\beta)\in(0,\infty)^{2}_{+}
. We denote

\displaystyle S_{\alpha,\beta}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{u\in U^{\prime}:\alpha\leq\left\lvert u\right\rvert_{U^{\prime}}\leq\beta\},

\displaystyle S_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{u\in U^{\prime}:\alpha\leq\left\lvert u\right\rvert_{U^{\prime}}\}.

By
\mathscr{A}_{1}(\alpha,\beta)
, respectively
\mathscr{A}_{2}(\alpha)
, we will denote the set of all closed subsets
A
of
U^{\prime}\setminus\{0\}
such that

A\subset S_{\alpha,\beta}\mbox{ or respectively, }A\subset S_{\alpha}.

(iii)

We put

\mathscr{A}_{1}\coloneqq\bigcup_{(\alpha,\beta)\in(0,\infty)^{2}_{+}}\mathscr{A}_{1}(\alpha,\beta).

We will now present the main result of the subsection,
which is our version of
[
39
, Lemma 6.1.11]
, a proof of which will be the focus of the remainder of this subsection.

Proposition 6.20
.

Let
A\in\mathscr{A}_{0}
. Then, the process
\overline{N}_{A}
defined by

\overline{N}_{A}(t)\coloneqq\sum\limits_{0<s\leq t}\mathds{1}_{A}(\Delta\overline{M}(s))-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\mathds{1}_{A}(F(s,\overline{u}(s),y))\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s,\;\;t\in[0,T],

(6.32)

is an
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}
-martingale.

Let us choose and fix
\beta>\alpha>0
. We also fix a set
A\in\mathscr{A}_{1}(\alpha,\beta)
.

Consider a sequence
(a_{k})_{k=2}^{\infty}
of continuous and bounded functions on
U^{\prime}
defined by

a_{k}(u)\coloneqq f\left(\frac{k^{2}\rho(u,A)^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right),\;\;u\in U^{\prime},

(6.33)

where

f:[0,\infty)\to[0,1])
is a
C^{1}
-class decreasing function such that

\mathds{1}_{\{0\}}\leq f\leq\mathds{1}_{[0,\frac{3}{4})}.

and
\rho(u,A)
is the
U^{\prime}
-distance of the vector
u\in U^{\prime}
from the set
A
.

Note that
a_{k}(u)\in[0,1]
for every
u\in U^{\prime}
and
a_{k}(u)=0
if
\rho(u,A)\geq\frac{\sqrt{3}\alpha}{2k}
.

Let us show few more properties of the functions
a_{k}
, which will be used later.

Lemma 6.21
.

Functions
a_{k}
defined in (
6.33
) satisfy

(i)

a_{k}\searrow\mathds{1}_{A}\mbox{ as }k\to\infty
.

(ii)

The support of
a_{k}
is contained in
S_{\frac{\alpha}{2}}
.

(iii)

For future reference we also show that

a_{k}(u)\leq\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}\left\lvert u\right\rvert_{U^{\prime}}\right)^{\ell}\qquad\mbox{ for all }u\in U^{\prime},k\geq 2,\ell\geq 1.

(6.34)

(iv)

For
u_{1},u_{2}\in U^{\prime}

|a_{k}(u_{2})-a_{k}(u_{1})|\leq\frac{k^{2}}{a^{2}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Lip}}\left\lvert u_{1}-u_{2}\right\rvert_{U^{\prime}}\bigl(\rho(u_{1},A)+\rho(u_{2},A)\bigr),

(6.35)

where
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Lip}}
is the Lipschitz constant of the function
f
.

Let us note that inequality (
6.34
) with
\ell=2
is used in the proof of Lemma
6.23
.

Proof.

Points (i) and (ii) are obvious. We show (iii). Indeed, if
u
is such that
\left\lvert u\right\rvert_{U^{\prime}}\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}
, then inequality (
6.34
) follows from

a_{k}(u)\leq 1\leq\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}\left\lvert u\right\rvert_{U^{\prime}}\right)^{\ell}\mbox{ for all }u\in U^{\prime},k\geq 2,\ell\geq 1.

On the other hand, if
u
is such that
\left\lvert u\right\rvert_{U^{\prime}}<\frac{\alpha}{2}
, then
\rho(u,A)\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}
because
A\subset S_{\alpha}
. This implies that
\frac{k^{2}\rho(u,A^{2})}{\alpha^{2}}\geq 1
for all
k\geq 2
and thus
a_{k}(u)=0
.

We show (iv). Let us observe that because of the following property of the distance function
\rho(\cdot,A)

\left\lvert\rho(x,A)-\rho(y,A)\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert x-y\right\rvert_{U^{\prime}},\;x,y\in U^{\prime},

(6.36)

we may estimate

\displaystyle|a_{k}(u_{2})-a_{k}(u_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k^{2}}{a^{2}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Lip}}\left\lvert\rho(u_{2},A)^{2}-\rho(u_{1},A)^{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k^{2}}{a^{2}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Lip}}\left\lvert u_{1}-u_{2}\right\rvert_{U^{\prime}}\bigl(\rho(u_{1},A)+\rho(u_{2},A)\bigr).

∎

Lemma 6.22
.

Let
(a_{k})_{k\geq 2}
be the sequence defined in (
6.33
).
Let
z\in\mathscr{Z}_{T}
and
(z_{n})
be a
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
-valued sequence such that

\displaystyle z_{n}\to z\mbox{ in }\mathscr{Z}_{T}\mbox{ as $n\to\infty$}.

(6.37)

Then, for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
and every integer
k>2
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}a_{k}(P_{m}F(s,z_{n}(s),y))\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

(6.38)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}a_{k}(P_{m}F(s,z(s),y))\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}a_{k}(P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y))\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

(6.39)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}a_{k}(F(s,z(s),y))\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Proof of Lemma
6.22
.

The proof of convergence (
6.38
) is similar, in fact easier than, to the proof convergence (
6.39
) and hence we will only
provide a detailed proof of the latter. For this purpose, we shall closely follow the proof of
[
39
, Lemma 6.1.7]
. Let us first observe since

z_{n}\to z
in
\mathscr{Z}_{T}
, then also
z_{n}\to z
in
\mathbb{D}([0,T];H_{w})
and hence by
[
14
, Lemma 2]

z_{n}
and
z
satisfy the following a priori estimates

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\bigl|z_{n}(s){\bigr|}_{H}^{2}+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\bigl|z(s){\bigr|}_{H}^{2}<\infty.

(6.40)

Let

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} D_{n}&\coloneqq\left\{(t,y)\in[0,T]\times Y:\rho(P_{n}F(t,z_{n}(t),y)),A)\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}\right\},\\
D&\coloneqq\left\{(t,y)\in[0,T]\times Y:\rho(F(t,z(t),y),A)\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}\right\}.\end{aligned}

(6.41)

It follows from the definition (
6.33
) of the functions
a_{k}
that for every
k\geq 2

(s,y)\in D_{n}^{c}\implies a_{k}(P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y))=0

and

(s,y)\in D^{c}\implies a_{k}(F(s,z(s),y))=0.

(6.42)

Therefore we can estimate by (
6.35
):

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}a_{k}(P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y))-a_{k}(F(s,z(s),y))\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k^{2}}{a^{2}}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Lip}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\left\lvert\rho(P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)),A)^{2}-\rho(F(s,z(s),y),A)^{2}\right\rvert\mathds{1}_{D_{n}\cap D}(s,y)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}a_{k}(P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y))\mathds{1}_{D_{n}\cap D^{c}}(s,y)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}a_{k}(F(s,z(s),y))\mathds{1}_{D_{n}^{c}\cap D}(s,y)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

(6.43)

\displaystyle\quad=:\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\mathrm{Lip}}\frac{k^{2}}{a^{2}}I_{n}^{1}+I_{n}^{2}+I_{n}^{3}

Note that for all
u\in U^{\prime}

\mbox{ if }\rho(u,A)\leq\frac{\alpha}{2},\mbox{ then }\rho(u,A)\leq\left\lvert u\right\rvert_{U^{\prime}}.

(6.44)

Indeed, suppose by contradiction that
there exists
u_{0}\in U^{\prime}
such that
\rho(u_{0},A)\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}
and
{|u_{0}|}_{U^{\prime}}<\rho(u_{0},A)
. Since
A\subset S_{\alpha}
, we have that
\rho(0,A)\geq\alpha
. By applying the triangle inequality we infer that

\displaystyle\alpha\leq\rho(0,A)\leq\rho(0,u_{0})+\rho(u_{0},A)={|u_{0}|}_{U^{\prime}}+\rho(u_{0},A)<2\rho(u_{0},A)\leq\alpha,

which is a contradiction thanks to the strict inequality.

Note that by the definition (
6.41
) of the set
D_{n}
and by (
6.44
) we have

\rho(P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y),A)\leq\left\lvert P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)\right\rvert_{U^{\prime}}\quad\mbox{ for $(s,y)\in D_{n}$}.

(6.45)

Also, by the inequalities (
6.36
) and (
6.45
) we infer that for
(s,y)\in D_{n}\cap D

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\left\lvert\rho(P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y),A)^{2}-\rho(F(s,z(s),y),A)^{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\rho(P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y),A)-\rho(F(s,z(s),y),A)\right\rvert\left(\rho(P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y),A)+\rho(F(s,z(s),y),A)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)-F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-140.45483pt\hskip 140.45483pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-140.45483pt\hskip 140.45483ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)-F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-140.45483pt\hskip 140.45483pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-140.45483pt\hskip 140.45483ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)-F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-101.69792pt\hskip 101.69792pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-101.69792pt\hskip 101.69792ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)-F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-90.45398pt\hskip 90.45398pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-90.45398pt\hskip 90.45398ptU^{\prime}}}}\left(\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-59.86458pt\hskip 59.86458pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-59.86458pt\hskip 59.86458ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-53.86452pt\hskip 53.86452pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-53.86452pt\hskip 53.86452ptU^{\prime}}}}+\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-61.99069pt\hskip 61.99069pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-61.99069pt\hskip 61.99069ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-61.99069pt\hskip 61.99069pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-61.99069pt\hskip 61.99069ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.88889pt\hskip 43.88889pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.88889pt\hskip 43.88889ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-38.81169pt\hskip 38.81169pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-38.81169pt\hskip 38.81169ptU^{\prime}}}}\right).

Using this together with the Hölder inequality we can estimate the term
I_{n}^{1}
as follows:

\displaystyle I_{n}^{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)-F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-140.45483pt\hskip 140.45483pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-140.45483pt\hskip 140.45483ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)-F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-140.45483pt\hskip 140.45483pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-140.45483pt\hskip 140.45483ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)-F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-101.69792pt\hskip 101.69792pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-101.69792pt\hskip 101.69792ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)-F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-90.45398pt\hskip 90.45398pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-90.45398pt\hskip 90.45398ptU^{\prime}}}}\mathds{1}_{D_{n}\cap D}(s,y)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\biggl(\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-59.86458pt\hskip 59.86458pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-59.86458pt\hskip 59.86458ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-53.86452pt\hskip 53.86452pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-53.86452pt\hskip 53.86452ptU^{\prime}}}}\mathds{1}_{D_{n}\cap D}(s,y)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-61.99069pt\hskip 61.99069pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-61.99069pt\hskip 61.99069ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-61.99069pt\hskip 61.99069pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-61.99069pt\hskip 61.99069ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.88889pt\hskip 43.88889pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.88889pt\hskip 43.88889ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|F(s,z(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-38.81169pt\hskip 38.81169pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-38.81169pt\hskip 38.81169ptU^{\prime}}}}\mathds{1}_{D_{n}\cap D}(s,y)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\biggr)

Since the linear map
P_{n}
is a contraction in
H
, see (
2.50
), by the linear growth condition (
3.5
) in Assumption F.
2
,
by the continuity of the embedding
{H}\hookrightarrow U^{\prime}
, see (
2.32
),
and by
[
14
, Lemma 2]
, there exists a constants
c>0
and
C_{2}>0
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639pt2}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.68639pt\hskip 80.68639ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-59.86458pt\hskip 59.86458pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-59.86458pt\hskip 59.86458ptU^{\prime}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|P_{n}F(s,z_{n}(s),y)|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-53.86452pt\hskip 53.86452pt2}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-53.86452pt\hskip 53.86452ptU^{\prime}}}}\mathds{1}_{D_{n}\cap D}(s,y)\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s
