-finite measure on
(X,\mathscr{B}_{X})
.

If
U\in\Gamma_{p}
, the process

\tilde{\eta}_{p}(t,U):=\eta_{p}((0,t]\times U)-\widehat{\eta}_{p}(t,U),\;\;t>0,

(C.16)

is an
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
-martingale.
The family
\widehat{\eta}_{p}(t,U)
is called the
compensator
of the point process
p
, or the
compensator
of the integer valued random measure
\eta_{p}
.

Let us now make some comments.

Remark C.32
.

If a random measure
\eta
is generated by a point process
p
, then the 4th bullet point in Definition
C.15
and Definition
C.31
are very similar but not identical. We don’t know if they are equivalent. First of all, according to Definition
C.27
,
point process
p:\Omega\to\Pi_{X}
is
\sigma
-finite if and only if the associated random measure is
\sigma
-finite. Secondly,
if
\widehat{\eta}
a
\sigma
-finite random measure such that conditions (ii) and (iii) of the former are satisfied, then we can define a family
\widehat{\eta}_{p}=\Bigl\{\widehat{\eta}_{p}(t,U):\,t>0,\;U\in\Gamma_{p}\Bigr\}
by

\widehat{\eta}_{p}(t,U):=\widehat{\eta}\bigl((0,t]\times U\bigr),t>0,\;U\in\Gamma_{p}

and prove that this family satisfies conditions (i), (ii) and (iii) of the latter.
We believe the following equivalence:

p
is point process of class
(QL)
according to Definition
C.32
if and only if the random measure
\eta_{p}
is of class
(QL)
according to Definition
C.15
.
The implication ‘
\Leftarrow
’ is obvious.
For ‘
\Rightarrow
’ we’d need to show that
\hat{\eta}((0,t]\times U):=\hat{\eta}_{p}(t,U)
extends to a
\sigma
-finite random measure on
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\otimes\mathscr{B}_{X}
.

Remark C.33
.

In
[
34
, Section I.9]
the authors use the notation
\mathscr{B}(X)
instead of an earlier notation
\mathscr{B}_{X}
. Moreover, they use a symbol
\eta_{p}(t,U)
instead of
\eta_{p}((0,t]\times U)
. We will continue to use the former notation.

Definition C.34
.

Suppose that
(X,\mathscr{B}_{X})
is a non-empty measure space and

\mathfrak{A}=\bigl(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P},\mathbb{F}\bigr),\mbox{ with }\mathbb{F}=(\mathscr{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0},

is a filtered probability space.
A point process
p:\Omega\to\Pi_{X}

(on
X
defined on
\Omega
) is called an
\mathbb{F}
-Poisson point process if and only if it is

\mathbb{F}
-adapted,
\sigma
-finite Poisson point process and, for every
t>0
, the random variables

\eta_{p}((0,t+h]\times U)-\eta_{p}((0,t]\times U),\;\;h>0,\;U\in\mathscr{B}_{X}

are independent of
\mathscr{F}_{t}
.

Remark C.35
.

A point process
p
is an
\mathbb{F}
-Poisson point process if the associated random measure
\eta
is an
\mathbb{F}
-Poisson random measure, see Definition
2.2
.

The following result is stated on page 60 of
[
34
]
.

Proposition C.36
.

Suppose that
(X,\mathscr{B}_{X})
is a non-empty measure space and

\mathfrak{A}=\bigl(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P},\mathbb{F}\bigr),\mbox{ with }\mathbb{F}=(\mathscr{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0},

is a filtered probability space. Assume that a point process
p:\Omega\to\Pi_{X}
(on
X
defined on
\Omega
) is an
\mathbb{F}
-Poisson point process. Then the following two conditions are equivalent.

The point process
p:\Omega\to\Pi_{X}
is of class
(QL)
(with respect to filtration
\mathbb{F}
).

For every
U\in\Gamma_{p}
, the function

(0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto\mathbb{E}\bigl[{\eta}_{p}((0,t]\times U)\bigr]

is continuous.

Corollary C.37
.

Suppose that
(X,\mathscr{B}_{X})
is a non-empty measure space and

\mathfrak{A}=\bigl(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P},\mathbb{F}\bigr),\mbox{ with }\mathbb{F}=(\mathscr{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0},

is a filtered probability space. Assume that

p:\Omega\to\Pi_{X}
is a stationary
\mathbb{F}
-Poisson point process
(on
X
defined on
\Omega
). Then
the point process
p:\Omega\to\Pi_{X}
is of class
(QL)
(with respect to filtration
\mathbb{F}
) with compensator

\widehat{\eta}_{p}(t,U):=tn(U),

where
n
is the characteristic measure of
p
, see
Definition
C.24
and
Proposition
C.25
.

A converse to the above result is given in
[
34
, Theorem I.6.2]
.

Appendix D
Representation Theorem for purely discontinuous martingales

In this section we state and prove a Representation Theorem for purely discontinuous martingales from Kallianpur and Xiong
[
39
, Theorem 3.4.7]
adapted to our needs (for Hilbert-space valued processes rather than processes in a conuclear space).
The proof relies on
[
39
, Theorem 3.4.6]
, which is taken from
[
34
, Theorem II.7.4]
. They write that the result goes back to the following papers: Grigelionis
[
33
]
(in Russian), Karoui-Lepeltier
[
29
]
(in Russian) and Tanaka
[
58
]
(in English).

Before we proceed we will formulate the following clear generalization of
[
34
, Theorem I.3.3.]
because the aforementioned Lemma II.7.1 from therein follows easily from it.

Theorem D.1
.

Assume that
(\mathbf{Z},\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Z}})
,
(\mathbf{X},\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}})
and
(\mathbf{Y},\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Y}})
are three measurable spaces.
Assume further that
(\mathbf{Z},\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Z}})
is a standard measurable space and that
m
is a non-negative measure on it.

Assume that a map

\displaystyle\theta\colon

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}\times\mathbf{Z}\to\mathbf{X}

is
\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Y}}\otimes\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Z}}/\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}}
-measurable. For each
y\in\mathbf{Y}
let us put
1
1

1

Let us observe that
\theta_{y}
is
\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Z}}/\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}}
-measurable.

\theta_{y}\coloneqq\theta(y,\cdot)\colon\mathbf{Z}\to\mathbf{X}
and let us assume that
q(y,\cdot)
is equal to the image of the measure
m
via
\theta_{y}
:

q(y,B)=m(\theta_{y}^{-1}(B)),\;\;B\in\mathbf{X}.

(D.1)

We finally assume that for every
B\in\mathbf{X}
the map

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}\ni y\mapsto q(y,B)\in[0,\infty]

(D.2)

is
\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Y}}
-measurable.

Then, there exists a function

\displaystyle Q\colon

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}\times\mathbf{X}\times\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to[0,\infty]

such that the following three conditions are satisfied.

for all
y\in\mathbf{Y}
and
x\in\mathbf{X}
, the map

\displaystyle Q(y,x,\cdot)\colon

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{Z}\to[0,\infty]

is a measure;

for every
A\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Z}}
the map

\displaystyle Q(\cdot,\cdot,A)\colon

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}\times\mathbf{X}\to[0,\infty]

is
\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Y}}
-measurable,

for all
y\in\mathbf{Y}
,
A\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Z}}
and
B\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}}
, the following equality holds:

\displaystyle m(A\cap\theta_{y}^{-1}(B))=\int_{\mathbf{X}}\mathds{1}_{B}(x)Q(y,x,A)\,q(y,\mathrm{d}x).

(D.3)

Remark D.2
.

Let us observe that in view of
(
D.1
)
and the change of measure Theorem the equality (
D.3
) takes the following equivalent form.

\displaystyle m(A\cap\theta_{y}^{-1}(B))

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathds{1}_{B}(\theta(y,z))Q(y,\theta(y,z),A)\,m(\mathrm{d}y).

(D.4)

On the other hand, the formula
[
34
, (II.7.28) from Lemma II.7.1]
is as follows. If

f\colon\mathbf{Y}\times\mathbf{X}\to[0,\infty]

is
\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Z}}\otimes\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{Z}}
-measurable, then for every
y\in\mathbf{Y}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{Z}}f(\theta(y,z),z)m(\mathrm{d}z)=\int_{\mathbf{X}}\Bigl[\int_{\mathbf{Z}}f(x,z)Q(y,\theta(y,z),\mathrm{d}z)\Bigr]\,q(y,\mathrm{d}x).

(D.5)

Taking a function
f
defined by
f(x,z)=\mathds{1}_{B}(x)\mathds{1}_{A}(z)
we can prove that the last equality (
D.5
) reduces to
(
D.3
).

We will be using the following version of
[
34
, Theorem II.7.4]
rewritten in terms of a random measure in place of a point process.

Theorem D.3
.

Assume that

\mathfrak{A}=\bigl({\Omega},{\mathscr{F}},{\mathbb{P}},{\mathbb{F}}\bigr),\mbox{ where }{\mathbb{F}}=({\mathscr{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0},

is a filtered probability space. Assume that
(\mathbf{X},\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}})
is a measurable space and

\eta
is a random measure on
\mathfrak{A}
and
(\mathbf{X},\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}})
.
Assume that
\eta
is of class
(QL)
and there exists
an
\mathbb{F}
-predictable generalized process

q\colon(0,\infty)\times\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}}\times\Omega\to[0,\infty]

(D.6)

such that the compensator
\widehat{\eta}
of
\eta
satisfies, for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega\in\Omega

\displaystyle\widehat{\eta}\bigl((0,t]\times A;\omega\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{(0,t]}\int_{A}q(s,\mathrm{d}x,\omega)\,\mathrm{d}s,\mbox{ for all $t\in(0,\infty)$, $A\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}}$}.

(D.7)

Suppose that
(Y,\mathscr{Y})
is a standard measurable space and
m
is a
\sigma
-finite non-negative measure on it. Suppose also that

\theta\colon(0,\infty)\times Y\times\Omega\to\mathbf{X}^{\ast}\coloneqq\mathbf{X}\cup\{\partial\}

is another
\mathbb{F}
-predictable generalized process
such that for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega\in\Omega

m(\{u\in Y:\theta(t,u,\omega)\in A\})=q(t,A,\omega),\mbox{ for all }t\in(0,\infty),\;\;A\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}}.

(D.8)

Then there exists an extension
\bigl(\tilde{\mathfrak{A}},\pi\bigr)
of
\mathfrak{A}
,
where

\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}=\bigl(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathscr{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}},\tilde{\mathbb{F}}\bigr),\mbox{ with }\tilde{\mathbb{F}}=(\tilde{\mathscr{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0},

and there exists an
Y
-valued stationary Poisson point process
\tilde{p}
on
\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}
,
with the corresponding Poisson random measure denoted by
{\eta}_{\tilde{p}}
, such that

the characteristic measure of
\tilde{p}
is equal to the measure
m
,

and the following equality holds for

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost every

\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}

\displaystyle\eta\bigl((0,t]\times A,\pi(\tilde{\omega})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}(\mathds{1}_{A}\circ\theta)(s,u,\pi(\tilde{\omega}))\,{\eta}_{\tilde{p}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u)

(D.9)

\displaystyle=\#\bigl\{s\in\mathrm{Dom}({\tilde{p}(\tilde{\omega})})\cap(0,t]:\theta(s,{\tilde{p}}(s,\tilde{\omega}),\pi(\tilde{\omega}))\in A\bigr\},

\displaystyle\mbox{for all $t\in[0,\infty)$, $A\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}}$}.

Proof.

Theorem
D.3
follows from Theorem
[
34
, Theorem II.7.4]
.
∎

Remark D.4
.

Let us observe that in view of assumption (
D.8
) by the change of measure Theorem, identity (
D.7
) can be written in the following equivalent form:

\displaystyle\widehat{\eta}\bigl((0,t]\times A;\omega\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}(\mathds{1}_{A}\circ\theta)(s,u,\omega)\,m(\mathrm{d}u)\,\mathrm{d}s\quad\mbox{ for all }t\in(0,\infty),\,A\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}}.

(D.10)

Remark D.5
.

Let us also observe that (
D.8
) implies

\displaystyle q(t,A,\omega)

\displaystyle=\int_{Y}(\mathds{1}_{A}\circ\theta)(t,u,\omega)\,m(\mathrm{d}u),\mbox{ for all }t\in(0,\infty)\mbox{ and }A\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}}.

This implies that we do not have to assume the existence of the generalized process
q
. Moreover, if the generalized process
\theta
is
\mathbb{F}
-predictable, by the Fubini Theorem we infer the
\mathbb{F}
-predictability of the generalized process
q
.

This observation is reflected by the way we formulate our next result, i.e. Theorem
D.6
.

Now we will formulate and prove a small modification of
[
39
, Theorem 3.4.7]
. Note that by
[
61
, Prop. 2.2.2]
, see also
[
64
, Proposition 2.2.1.]
, every Banach space valued martingale has a càdlàg version, hence, without loss of generality, we can assume below that the martingale
M
is càdlàg.
The notation in the theorem below differs slightly from notation use in
[
39
]
. In particular,
we use notation
(Y,\mathscr{Y})
instead of
(\mathbf{U},\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{U}})
.

Theorem D.6
.

Assume that

\mathfrak{A}=\bigl({\Omega},{\mathscr{F}},{\mathbb{P}},{\mathbb{F}}\bigr),\mbox{ with }{\mathbb{F}}=({\mathscr{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0},

is a filtered probability space.
Assume that
\mathbf{X}
is a separable Banach space. Denote the Borel
\sigma
-field on
\mathbf{X}
(resp. on
\mathbf{X}\setminus\{0\}
) by
\mathscr{B}({\mathbf{X}})
(resp.
\mathscr{B}(\mathbf{X}\setminus\{0\})
).
Assume that
M=\bigl(M(t):\;t\geq 0\bigr)
is a square integrable
\mathbf{X}
-valued càdlàg,
purely discontinuous martingale defined on
\mathfrak{A}
.

Let
\eta_{M}
be an integer valued random measure on
(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbf{X}\setminus\{0\},\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\otimes\mathscr{B}(\mathbf{X}\setminus\{0\}))
and
\mathfrak{A}
associated with
M
, defined for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega\in\Omega
by

\eta_{M}\bigl((0,t]\times A;\omega\bigr)\coloneqq\sum_{s\in(0,t]}\mathds{1}_{A}(\Delta M(s,\omega))

(D.11)

for all
t\in[0,\infty)
,
A\in\mathscr{B}(\mathbf{X}\setminus\{0\})
.

2
2

2

Note that
\eta_{M}\bigl((0,t]\times A;\omega\bigr)=\#\bigl\{s\in(0,t]:\Delta M(s,\omega)\in A\bigr\}=\sum_{s>0}\mathds{1}_{(0,t]\times A}(s,\Delta M(s,\omega))
.

Assume that
\eta_{M}
is of class
(QL)
.

Assume also that
(Y,\mathscr{Y})
is a standard measurable space, see
[
34
, Definition I.3.3]
,

m
is a
\sigma
-finite non-negative measure defined on it and

\theta\colon[0,\infty)\times Y\times\Omega\to(\mathbf{X}\setminus\{0\})^{\ast}\coloneqq(\mathbf{X}\setminus\{0\})\cup\{\partial\}

is a generalized predictable process such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}|\theta(s,u)|^{2}m(\mathrm{d}u)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]<\infty.

(D.12)

Assume finally that the compensator
\widehat{\eta}_{M}
of
\eta_{M}
satisfies

\displaystyle\widehat{\eta}_{M}\bigl((0,t]\times A;\omega\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}m(\{u\in Y:\theta(s,u,\omega)\in A\})\,\mathrm{d}s\mbox{ for all }t>0,\,A\in\mathscr{B}(\mathbf{X}\setminus\{0\}).

(D.13)

Then there exists an extension

\bigl(\tilde{\mathfrak{A}},\pi\bigr)
of
\mathfrak{A}
, where

\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}=\bigl(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathscr{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}},\tilde{\mathbb{F}}\bigr),\mbox{ with }\tilde{\mathbb{F}}=(\tilde{\mathscr{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0},

and there exists a stationary
Y
-valued Poisson point process
\tilde{p}
on
\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}
,
with the corresponding the Poisson random measure denoted by
{\eta}_{\tilde{p}}
, such that

the characteristic measure of the stationary Poisson point process
\tilde{p}
is equal to the measure
m
, and

the following equality holds for
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost every

\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}

\displaystyle M(t,\pi(\tilde{\omega}))

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\theta(s,u,\pi(\tilde{\omega}))\,\tilde{{\eta}}_{\tilde{p}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u),\;\;\mbox{ for every }t\in[0,\infty).

(D.14)

Proof of Theorem
D.6
.

Step 1.
Let an object
\eta_{M}
be defined in (
D.11
).
According to
[
35
, Proposition II.1.16]
, where a proof is given only for
\mathbb{R}
-valued processes,
\eta_{M}
is an integer-valued random measure on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbf{X}
.

The assumptions imposed on the random measure
\eta_{M}
are the same as in Theorem
D.3
.
Hence by Theorem
D.3
there exists an extension

\bigl(\tilde{\mathfrak{A}},\pi\bigr)
,
where
\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}=\bigl(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathscr{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}},\tilde{\mathbb{F}}\bigr)
, with
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}=(\tilde{\mathscr{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
,
of
\mathfrak{A}
and there exists a stationary
Y
-valued stationary Poisson point process
\tilde{p}
on
\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}
,
with the corresponding Poisson random measure denoted by
{\eta}_{\tilde{p}}
, such that

the characteristic measure of
\tilde{p}
is equal to
m
, and

the following equality holds for every for
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost every
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}

\displaystyle\eta_{M}\bigl((0,t]\times A,\pi(\tilde{\omega})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}(\mathds{1}_{A}\circ\theta)(s,u,\pi(\tilde{\omega}))\,{\eta}_{\tilde{p}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u)

(D.15)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s>0}\mathds{1}_{(0,t]\times A}(s,\theta(s,{\tilde{p}}(s,\tilde{\omega}),\pi(\tilde{\omega})))

for all
t\in[0,\infty)
,
A\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbf{X}}

Step 2.
Note that the preimage by
\pi
of a subset
\Omega
of measure zero has measure zero in
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathscr{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
. From (
D.11
) we infer that

\displaystyle\eta_{M}\bigl((0,t]\times A;\pi(\tilde{\omega})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s>0}\mathds{1}_{(0,t]\times A}(s,\Delta M(s,\pi(\tilde{\omega})))

(D.16)

for all
t\in[0,\infty)
,
A\in\mathscr{B}(\mathbf{X}\setminus\{0\})
.
By applying Corollary
C.9

we infer that for
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost every
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}

\Delta M(t,\pi(\tilde{\omega}))=\theta(t,{\tilde{p}}(t,\tilde{\omega}),\pi(\tilde{\omega})),\;\;\mbox{ for every }t\in(0,\infty).

Let us next define a process
\tilde{M}
by formula (
B.2
), which by Proposition
B.5
, is a square-integrable purely discontinuous martingale on
\bigl(\tilde{\mathfrak{A}},\pi\bigr)
. Moreover, it satisfies for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega\in\Omega

\Delta\tilde{M}(t,\tilde{\omega})=\tilde{\theta}(t,{\tilde{p}}(t,\tilde{\omega}),\tilde{\omega}),\;\;\mbox{ for every }t\in(0,\infty),

where the process
\tilde{\theta}\colon[0,\infty)\times Y\times\tilde{\Omega}\to X\setminus\{0\}
is defined by (
B.1
).

Step 3.
Define an auxiliary process
\xi
on
\bigl(\tilde{\mathfrak{A}},\pi\bigr)
, by

\displaystyle\xi(t,\tilde{\omega})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\theta\bigl(s,u,\pi(\tilde{\omega})\bigr)\,\tilde{\eta}_{\tilde{p}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\tilde{\theta}\bigl(s,u,\tilde{\omega}\bigr)\,\tilde{\eta}_{\tilde{p}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u).

By the 2nd part of Lemma
B.3
the process

\tilde{\theta}
is a generalized predictable process. Moreover, by property (ii) in Definition
B.2
the change of measure theorem we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}|\tilde{\theta}(s,u)|^{2}m(\mathrm{d}u)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}|\theta(s,u)|^{2}m(\mathrm{d}u)\,\mathrm{d}s\right].

which is finite by assumptions.

Therefore, by
[
39
, Theorem 3.4.5]
we infer that
\xi
is a square integrable purely discontinuous martingale on probability space
\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}
and that for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega\in\Omega

\Delta\xi(t,\tilde{\omega})=\tilde{\theta}\bigl(t,{\tilde{p}}(t,\tilde{\omega}),\tilde{\omega}\bigr),\;\;\mbox{ for every }t\in(0,\infty).

We conclude that for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega\in\Omega

\Delta\tilde{M}(t,\tilde{\omega})=\Delta\xi(t,\tilde{\omega}),\;\;\mbox{ for every }t\in(0,\infty).

(D.17)

Step 4.
From (
D.17
) we deduce that the process
\tilde{M}-\xi
is square integrable continuous martingale on probability space
\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}
.
On the other hand, since both processes
\tilde{M}
and
\xi
are square-integrable purely discontinuous martingales and the set of all square-integrable purely discontinuous martingales is a vector space we infer that the process
\tilde{M}-\xi
is also a is a square-integrable purely discontinuous martingale. We conclude that
\tilde{M}-\xi
is equal to
0
. Hence we proved that for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega\in\Omega

\displaystyle\tilde{M}(t,\tilde{\omega})=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}\tilde{\theta}\bigl(s,u,\tilde{\omega}\bigr)\,\tilde{\eta}_{\tilde{p}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u),\;\;\;\;\mbox{ for every }t\in(0,\infty).

This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Appendix E
Filtration enlargement and martingales

Definition E.1
.

A process
\mathrm{M}\colon\Omega\times[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
is a martingale with respect to a filtration
\mathbb{H}=(\mathscr{H}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
if and only if it satisfies the following properties

(i)

For every
t\geq 0
the function
M_{t}\coloneqq M(\cdot,t)
is
\mathscr{H}_{t}
-measurable,

(ii)

For every
t\geq 0
the function
M_{t}
is integrable,

(iii)

For all pairs
(s,t)\in[0,\infty)^{2}_{+}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[M_{t}|\mathscr{H}_{s}\right]=M_{s},

(E.1)

i.e. for every
A\in\mathscr{H}_{s}

\int_{A}{M_{t}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{A}{M_{s}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}.

or

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathds{1}_{A}(M_{t}-M_{s})\bigr]=0.

Lemma E.2
.

Assume that
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P})
is a probability space
with the family of all
\mathbb{P}
-null sets of
\mathscr{F}
denoted by
\mathscr{N}
, i.e.

\mathscr{N}\coloneqq\bigl\{A\subset\Omega:\exists B\in\mathscr{F}:A\subset B\mbox{ and }\mathbb{P}(B)=0\bigr\}.

(E.2)

Assume that
\mathbb{H}
is the filtration
\mathbb{H}=(\mathscr{H}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
and
\mathbb{G}=(\mathscr{G}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is the usual augmentation of
\mathbb{H}
, i.e.

\mathscr{G}_{t}=\bigcap_{s>t}\tilde{\mathscr{H}_{s}},\,t\geq 0,

(E.3)

where
\tilde{\mathscr{H}_{s}}
is the
(\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P})
-completion of the

\sigma
-field
\mathscr{H}_{s}
, i.e.

\tilde{\mathscr{H}_{s}}\coloneqq\sigma(\mathscr{H}_{s}\cup\mathscr{N}),\,s\geq 0.

(E.4)

If a process
\mathrm{M}=\left(M_{t}:t\geq 0\right)
is a càdlàg
\mathbb{H}
-martingale on
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P})
, Then, it is also a
\mathbb{G}
-martingale.

Proof of Lemma
E.2
.

Let us choose and fix a probability space
(\Omega,\mathscr{P},\mathbb{P})
, a filtration
\mathbb{H}=(\mathscr{H}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
,
its augmentation
\mathbb{G}=(\mathscr{G}_{t})_{t\geq 0}

and an
\mathbb{H}
-martingale
\mathrm{M}
.

First of all let us observe that in view of
[
38
, Lemma 7.8]
, for every
t\geq 0
, the
\sigma
-field
\mathscr{G}_{t}
is equal to the
(\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P})
-completion
\tilde{\mathscr{H}_{t+}}
of the
the
\sigma
-field
\mathscr{H}_{t+}\coloneqq\bigcap_{s>t}\mathscr{H}_{s}
, i.e.

\mathscr{G}_{t}=\sigma\Bigl(\bigl(\bigcap_{s>t}\mathscr{H}_{s}\bigr)\cup\mathscr{N}\Bigr)=\tilde{\mathscr{H}_{t+}}.

(E.5)

Thus we infer that the filtration
(\mathscr{G}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
satisfies the usual conditions, especially it is right-continuous and complete.

We first notice that since
\mathrm{M}
is an
\mathbb{H}
-martingale, it satisfies the property (ii) of Definition
E.1
. Thus, we only need to check that
\mathrm{M}
satisfies conditions (i) and (iii) of that definition for the enlarged filtration
\mathbb{G}
.

We first check the condition (i) of Definition
E.1
. In order to do this let
t\in[0,\infty)
be arbitrary, but fixed. We observe that since
M_{t}
is
\mathscr{H}_{t}
-measurable, it is also
\mathscr{H}_{r}
-measurable for any
r>t
and hence, by (
E.3
),
\mathscr{G}_{t}
-measurable.

We now check the condition (iii) of Definition
E.1
. For this purpose we choose and fix an arbitrary pair
(s,t)\in[0,\infty)^{2}_{+}
with
s<t
.
We first prove that

\mathbb{E}[M_{t}|\mathscr{H}_{s+}]=M_{s}.

(E.6)

For this purpose let us consider a sequence
(s_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

s_{n}\in(s,t)\mbox{ for every $n\in\mathbb{R}$ and }s_{n}\searrow s.

(E.7)

Then we have

\mathscr{H}_{s+}=\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathscr{H}_{s_{n}}.

Therefore, by the conditioning limits Theorem, see
[
38
, Theorem 7.23]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[M_{t}|\mathscr{H}_{s+}]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[M_{t}|\mathscr{H}_{s_{n}}]\mbox{ a.s.\ and in }\mathbb{L}^{1}.

Since by assumptions,
M
is an
\mathbb{H}
-martingale we infer that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[M_{t}|\mathscr{H}_{s_{n}}]=M_{s_{n}}\mbox{ a.s.}.

Moreover, since by assumptions,
M
is a càdlàg process we infer that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}M_{s_{n}}=M_{s}\mbox{ a.s.}

Combining the last three equalities we conclude the proof of identity
(
E.6
).

To conclude the proof of Lemma
E.2
, in view of equality (
E.5
), it is sufficient to observe that
if
X
is
\mathscr{F}
-measurable integrable function and
\mathscr{H}
is a sub-
\sigma
-field of
\mathscr{F}
, then

\mathbb{E}[X|\mathscr{H}]=\mathbb{E}[X|\overline{\mathscr{H}}].

(E.8)

Obviously, the random variable
Y\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[X|\mathscr{H}]
being
\mathscr{H}
-measurable is also

\overline{\mathscr{H}}
-measurable. We only need to prove, with
Z=X-Y
, that

\mathbb{E}\bigl[]\mathds{1}_{A}Z\bigr]=0\mbox{ for every }A\in\overline{\mathscr{H}}.

Let us choose and fix a set
A\in\overline{\mathscr{H}}
. Then by
[
38
, Lemma 7.25]
there exist two sets
A_{\pm}\in\mathscr{H}
with

\displaystyle A_{-}\subset A\subset A_{+}\mbox{ and }\mathbb{P}(A_{+}\setminus A_{-})=0.

Therefore
\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathds{1}_{A+}Z\bigr]=0
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathds{1}_{A-}Z\bigr]=0
and because
\mathds{1}_{A\setminus A_{-}}Z=0
a.s. and
\mathds{1}_{A_{+}\setminus A}=0
a.s.

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathds{1}_{A\setminus A_{-}}Z\bigr]=0\mbox{ and }\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathds{1}_{A_{+}\setminus A}Z\bigr]=0.

Since
Z
is integrable, we infer that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathds{1}_{A}Z\bigr]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigl\{\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathds{1}_{A_{+}}Z\bigr]+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathds{1}_{A_{-}}Z\bigr]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathds{1}_{A_{+}\setminus A}Z\bigr]+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathds{1}_{A\setminus A_{-}}Z\bigr]\bigr\}=\frac{1}{2}\times 0=0.

∎

Appendix F
A small strengthening of Lemma B.1 from paper
[
17
]

We will formulate and prove a slightly stronger version of
[
17
, Lemma B.1]
.

Lemma F.1
.

Assume that
u\in{L}^{2}(0,T;H)
and
{({u}_{n})}_{n}
is a bounded sequence in
{L}^{2}(0,T;H)
such that

in
{L}^{2}(0,T;{H}_{\mathrm{loc}})
. If

,
then for every

,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}|\mathchoice{{\hskip 10.67899pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n}(s),{u}_{n}(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-86.00594pt\hskip (F.1)

Proof.

. Then there exists

.
Then, by inequality (

) we infer that for every

(F.2)

Since,

B({u}_{n},{u}_{n})-B(u,u)=B({u}_{n}-u,{u}_{n})+B(u,{u}_{n}-u)
.
by using the estimate (

) and the Hőlder inequality, we infer that there exists

such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n}(s),{u}_{n}(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-86.00594pt\hskip 86.00594pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-86.00594pt\hskip 86.00594pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n}(s),{u}_{n}(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-86.00594pt\hskip 86.00594pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-86.00594pt\hskip 86.00594pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n}(s),{u}_{n}(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-72.4319pt\hskip 72.4319pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-72.4319pt\hskip 72.4319pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n}(s),{u}_{n}(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-66.81938pt\hskip 66.81938pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-66.81938pt\hskip 66.81938pt}}}-\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-76.7859pt\hskip 76.7859pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-76.7859pt\hskip 76.7859pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-76.7859pt\hskip 76.7859pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-76.7859pt\hskip 76.7859pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-62.62392pt\hskip 62.62392pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-62.62392pt\hskip 62.62392pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-57.0114pt\hskip 57.0114pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-57.0114pt\hskip 57.0114pt}}}\Bigr|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n}(s)-u(s),{u}_{n}(s)\bigr)\ ,\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-116.97348pt\hskip 116.97348pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-116.97348pt\hskip 116.97348pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n}(s)-u(s),{u}_{n}(s)\bigr)\ ,\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-116.97348pt\hskip 116.97348pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-116.97348pt\hskip 116.97348pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n}(s)-u(s),{u}_{n}(s)\bigr)\ ,\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-93.976pt\hskip 93.976pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-93.976pt\hskip 93.976pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n}(s)-u(s),{u}_{n}(s)\bigr)\ ,\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-85.85896pt\hskip 85.85896pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-85.85896pt\hskip 85.85896pt}}}\Bigr|\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u(s),{u}_{n}(s)-u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-106.53015pt\hskip 106.53015pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-106.53015pt\hskip 106.53015pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u(s),{u}_{n}(s)-u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-106.53015pt\hskip 106.53015pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-106.53015pt\hskip 106.53015pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u(s),{u}_{n}(s)-u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-84.97478pt\hskip 84.97478pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-84.97478pt\hskip 84.97478pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u(s),{u}_{n}(s)-u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-77.27437pt\hskip 77.27437pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-77.27437pt\hskip 77.27437pt}}}\Bigr|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t}|{u}_{n}(s)-u(s){|}_{H_{R}}|{u}_{n}(s){|}_{H_{R}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}|u(s){|}_{H_{R}}|{u}_{n}(s)-u(s){|}_{H_{R}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr)\cdot\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|\psi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.51382pt\hskip 16.51382pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.51382pt\hskip 16.51382pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|\psi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.51382pt\hskip 16.51382pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.51382pt\hskip 16.51382pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|\psi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.0417pt\hskip 10.0417pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.0417pt\hskip 10.0417pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|\psi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.23622pt\hskip 9.23622pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.23622pt\hskip 9.23622pt{V}_{s}}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{u}_{n}-u\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H_{R})}\bigl(\|u_{n}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H_{R})}+\|u\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H_{R})}\bigr)\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|\psi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.51382pt\hskip 16.51382pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.51382pt\hskip 16.51382pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|\psi|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.51382pt\hskip 16.51382pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.51382pt\hskip 16.51382pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|\psi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.0417pt\hskip 10.0417pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.0417pt\hskip 10.0417pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|\psi|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.23622pt\hskip 9.23622pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.23622pt\hskip 9.23622pt{V}_{s}}}},

Since
{u}_{n}\to u
in
{L}^{2}(0,T;{H}_{\mathrm{loc}})
, we infer that (
F.1
) holds for every
\psi\in\mathscr{V}
.

If
\psi\in{V}_{\gamma}
then for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists
{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\in\mathscr{V}
such that
\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|\psi-{\psi}_{\varepsilon}|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.6392pt\hskip 30.6392pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.6392pt\hskip 30.6392pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|\psi-{\psi}_{\varepsilon}|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.6392pt\hskip 30.6392pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.6392pt\hskip 30.6392pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|\psi-{\psi}_{\varepsilon}|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-21.90114pt\hskip 21.90114pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-21.90114pt\hskip 21.90114pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|\psi-{\psi}_{\varepsilon}|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-20.12349pt\hskip 20.12349pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-20.12349pt\hskip 20.12349pt{V}_{s}}}}\leq\varepsilon
.
Then, for every
s\in[0,T]
, with
u_{n}={u}_{n}(s)
and
u=u(s)
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n},u_{n}\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-101.41663pt\hskip 101.41663pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-101.41663pt\hskip 101.41663pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n},u_{n}\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-101.41663pt\hskip 101.41663pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-101.41663pt\hskip 101.41663pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n},u_{n}\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-82.31946pt\hskip 82.31946pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-82.31946pt\hskip 82.31946pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl({u}_{n},u_{n}\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-76.6776pt\hskip 76.6776pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-76.6776pt\hskip 76.6776pt}}}\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u\bigr),\psi-{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-130.22948pt\hskip 130.22948pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-130.22948pt\hskip 130.22948pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u\bigr),\psi-{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-130.22948pt\hskip 130.22948pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-130.22948pt\hskip 130.22948pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u\bigr),\psi-{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-104.20325pt\hskip 104.20325pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-104.20325pt\hskip 104.20325pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u\bigr),\psi-{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-96.3323pt\hskip 96.3323pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-96.3323pt\hskip 96.3323pt}}}\bigr|+\bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-106.24144pt\hskip 106.24144pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-106.24144pt\hskip 106.24144pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-98.08575pt\hskip 98.08575pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-98.08575pt\hskip 98.08575pt}}}\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(\bigl|B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}\bigr){\bigr|}_{{V}_{\gamma}^{\prime}}+\bigl|B\bigl(u,u\bigr){\bigr|}_{{V}_{\gamma}^{\prime}}\bigr)\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{|\psi-\psi_{\varepsilon}|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.86137pt\hskip 32.86137pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.86137pt\hskip 32.86137pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{|\psi-\psi_{\varepsilon}|}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.86137pt\hskip 32.86137pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.86137pt\hskip 32.86137pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{|\psi-\psi_{\varepsilon}|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-21.90114pt\hskip 21.90114pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-21.90114pt\hskip 21.90114pt{V}_{s}}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{|\psi-\psi_{\varepsilon}|}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-20.12349pt\hskip 20.12349pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-20.12349pt\hskip 20.12349pt{V}_{s}}}}

\displaystyle+\bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-106.24144pt\hskip 106.24144pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-106.24144pt\hskip 106.24144pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-98.08575pt\hskip 98.08575pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-98.08575pt\hskip 98.08575pt}}}\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\bigl(|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{2}+|u(s){|}_{H}^{2}\bigr)+\bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-128.95874pt\hskip 128.95874pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-106.24144pt\hskip 106.24144pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-106.24144pt\hskip 106.24144pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n},u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u,u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-98.08575pt\hskip 98.08575pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-98.08575pt\hskip 98.08575pt}}}\bigr|.

Hence

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-122.41687pt\hskip 122.41687pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-122.41687pt\hskip 122.41687pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-111.74736pt\hskip 111.74736pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-111.74736pt\hskip 111.74736pt}}}\bigr|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\bigl(|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{2}+|u(s){|}_{H}^{2}\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}s+\bigl|\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-153.88934pt\hskip 153.88934pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-153.88934pt\hskip 153.88934pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-153.88934pt\hskip 153.88934pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-153.88934pt\hskip 153.88934pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-126.29015pt\hskip 126.29015pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-126.29015pt\hskip 126.29015pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-115.62064pt\hskip 115.62064pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-115.62064pt\hskip 115.62064pt}}}\,\mathrm{d}s\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\cdot\bigl(\sup_{n\geq 1}\|u_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\|u\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}\bigr)+\int_{0}^{T}\bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-153.88934pt\hskip 153.88934pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-153.88934pt\hskip 153.88934pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-153.88934pt\hskip 153.88934pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-153.88934pt\hskip 153.88934pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-126.29015pt\hskip 126.29015pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-126.29015pt\hskip 126.29015pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),{\psi}_{\varepsilon}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-115.62064pt\hskip 115.62064pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-115.62064pt\hskip 115.62064pt}}}\bigr|\,\mathrm{d}s.

Passing to the upper limit as
n\to\infty
, we obtain

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-122.41687pt\hskip 122.41687pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-122.41687pt\hskip 122.41687pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-111.74736pt\hskip 111.74736pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-111.74736pt\hskip 111.74736pt}}}\bigr|\,\mathrm{d}s\leq M\varepsilon,

where
M\coloneqq\sup_{n\geq 1}\|u_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\|u\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}<\infty
.
Since
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, we infer that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\bigl|\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.27783pt\hskip 151.27783pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-122.41687pt\hskip 122.41687pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-122.41687pt\hskip 122.41687pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle B\bigl(u_{n}(s),u_{n}(s)\bigr)-B\bigl(u(s),u(s)\bigr),\psi\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-111.74736pt\hskip 111.74736pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-111.74736pt\hskip 111.74736pt}}}\bigr|\,\mathrm{d}s=0.

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix G
Proof of Lemma
4.2

Let us choose and fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
{u}_{n}=\bigl({u}_{n}(t){\bigr)}_{t\in[0,T]}
be the the processes from Lemma
4.1
.
For every all
R>0
let us define

{{\tau}_{n}}(R):=\inf\{t\geq 0:\,\,|{u}_{n}(t){|}_{H}>R\}\wedge T.

(G.1)

Since the process
{u}_{n}
is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted and right-continuous,
and the filtration
\mathbb{F}
is right-continuous,
by Proposition 2.1.6 from
[
30
]
, we infer that

{\tau}_{n}(R)
is an
\mathbb{F}
-stopping time. Moreover, since it is càdlàg on
[0,T]
, the trajectories
[0,T]\ni t\mapsto{u}_{n}(t)
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. Thus

{\tau}_{n}(R)\nearrow T
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., as
R\to\infty
. In fact,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. there exits (a random)
R_{0}>0
such that

{\tau}_{n}(R)=T
for all
R\geq R_{0}
.

Assume that
p=2
or
p=4+\gamma
.
Using the Itô formula,
see
[
34
, Th. II.5.1]
,
to the function

\phi(x):=|x{|}^{p}:=|x{|}_{H}^{p},\;\;x\in H,

we obtain for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle|{u}_{n}({t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}){|}^{p}=|P_{n}{u}_{0}{|}^{p}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\bigl\{p|u_{n}(s){|}^{p-2}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),-{\mathscr{A}}_{n}u_{n}(s)-{B}_{n}(u_{n}(s))+P_{n}f(s)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-168.50739pt\hskip 168.50739pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-168.50739pt\hskip 168.50739pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),-{\mathscr{A}}_{n}u_{n}(s)-{B}_{n}(u_{n}(s))+P_{n}f(s)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-168.50739pt\hskip 168.50739pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-168.50739pt\hskip 168.50739pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),-{\mathscr{A}}_{n}u_{n}(s)-{B}_{n}(u_{n}(s))+P_{n}f(s)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-134.6627pt\hskip 134.6627pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-134.6627pt\hskip 134.6627pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),-{\mathscr{A}}_{n}u_{n}(s)-{B}_{n}(u_{n}(s))+P_{n}f(s)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-121.8312pt\hskip 121.8312pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-121.8312pt\hskip 121.8312pt}}}\bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\int_{{Y}}\big\{\phi\bigl(u_{n}(s-)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),y)\bigr)-\phi\bigl(u_{n}(s-)\bigr)\bigr\}\,\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\int_{{Y}}\big\{\phi\bigl(u_{n}(s))+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)\bigr)-\phi\bigl(u_{n}(s)\bigr)

\displaystyle-\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle\phi^{\prime}(u_{n}(s)),P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-123.71788pt\hskip 123.71788pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-123.71788pt\hskip 123.71788pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle\phi^{\prime}(u_{n}(s)),P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-123.71788pt\hskip 123.71788pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-123.71788pt\hskip 123.71788pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle\phi^{\prime}(u_{n}(s)),P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-99.1963pt\hskip 99.1963pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-99.1963pt\hskip 99.1963pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle\phi^{\prime}(u_{n}(s)),P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-89.90657pt\hskip 89.90657pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-89.90657pt\hskip 89.90657pt}}}\bigr\}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y).

By (
2.58
) and (
2.57
) we obtain for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle|{u}_{n}({t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}){|}^{p}=|P_{n}{u}_{0}{|}^{p}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\bigl\{-p|u_{n}(s){|}^{p-2}{\|u_{n}(s)\|}^{2}+p|u_{n}(s){|}^{p-2}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),f(s)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-54.29298pt\hskip 54.29298pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-54.29298pt\hskip 54.29298pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),f(s)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-54.29298pt\hskip 54.29298pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-54.29298pt\hskip 54.29298pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),f(s)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.40654pt\hskip 44.40654pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.40654pt\hskip 44.40654pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),f(s)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.47144pt\hskip 40.47144pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.47144pt\hskip 40.47144pt}}}\bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\int_{{Y}}\big\{|u_{n}(s-)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),y){|}^{p}-|u_{n}({s}^{-}){|}^{p}\bigr\}\,\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\int_{{Y}}\big\{|u_{n}(s)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y){|}^{p}-|u_{n}(s){|}^{p}

\displaystyle-p|u_{n}(s){|}^{p-2}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-107.17618pt\hskip 107.17618pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-107.17618pt\hskip 107.17618pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-107.17618pt\hskip 107.17618pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-107.17618pt\hskip 107.17618pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-85.43306pt\hskip 85.43306pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-85.43306pt\hskip 85.43306pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n}(s),P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-77.5766pt\hskip 77.5766pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-77.5766pt\hskip 77.5766pt}}}\bigr\}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, for
f\in V^{\prime}
and
u_{n}\in V
, we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle f,u_{n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.80682pt\hskip 28.80682pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.80682pt\hskip 28.80682pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle f,u_{n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.80682pt\hskip 28.80682pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.80682pt\hskip 28.80682pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle f,u_{n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.92728pt\hskip 23.92728pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.92728pt\hskip 23.92728pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle f,u_{n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.58932pt\hskip 22.58932pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.58932pt\hskip 22.58932pt}}}\leq\,{|f|}_{V^{\prime}}\cdot{\|u_{n}\|}_{V}\leq\frac{1}{2}\bigl\{{|f|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+|u_{n}{|}_{H}^{2}+{\|u_{n}\|}^{2}\bigr\}.

Thus we infer that

\displaystyle p{|u_{n}|}^{p-2}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle u_{n},f\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.36238pt\hskip 29.36238pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.36238pt\hskip 29.36238pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle u_{n},f\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.36238pt\hskip 29.36238pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.36238pt\hskip 29.36238pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n},f\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.35783pt\hskip 24.35783pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.35783pt\hskip 24.35783pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n},f\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.93655pt\hskip 22.93655pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.93655pt\hskip 22.93655pt}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p}{2}{|u_{n}|}^{p-2}{\|u_{n}\|}^{2}+\frac{p}{2}{|u_{n}|}^{p}+\frac{p}{2}{|f|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}{|u_{n}|}^{p-2}.

Since by the Young inequality

\displaystyle\frac{p}{2}{|f|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}{|u_{n}|}^{p-2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p}{2}\Bigl\{\frac{2}{p}{|f|}_{V^{\prime}}^{p}+\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{p}\Bigr){|u_{n}|}^{p}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle={|f|}_{V^{\prime}}^{p}+\Bigl(\frac{p}{2}-1\Bigr){|u_{n}|}^{p},

we obtain

\displaystyle p{|u_{n}|}^{p-2}\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle u_{n},f\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.36238pt\hskip 29.36238pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.36238pt\hskip 29.36238pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle u_{n},f\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.36238pt\hskip 29.36238pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.36238pt\hskip 29.36238pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n},f\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.35783pt\hskip 24.35783pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.35783pt\hskip 24.35783pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle u_{n},f\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.93655pt\hskip 22.93655pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.93655pt\hskip 22.93655pt}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p}{2}{|u_{n}|}^{p-2}{\|u_{n}\|}^{2}+(p-1){|u_{n}|}^{p}+{|f|}_{V^{\prime}}^{p}.

Thus,
applying the above for
f=f(s)
and
u_{n}=u_{n}(s)
, by Assumption A
1
, (
2.16
), we obtain for all
s\in[0,T\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)]
, with
c_{n}=|P_{n}u_{0}{|}^{p}+\int_{0}^{T}{|f(s)|}_{V^{\prime}}^{p}\,ds
and
c_{1}=p-1
, we have

\displaystyle|{u}_{n}({t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}){|}^{p}+\frac{p}{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}|u_{n}(s){|}^{p-2}{\|u_{n}(s)\|}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

(G.2)

\displaystyle\leq c_{n}+c_{1}\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}|u_{n}(s){|}^{p}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\int_{{Y}}\big\{|u_{n}(s-)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),y){|}^{p}-|u_{n}(s-){|}^{p}\bigr\}\,\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\int_{Y}\big\{|u_{n}(s)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y){|}^{p}-|u_{n}(s){|}^{p}

\displaystyle-p|u_{n}(s){|}^{p-2}{(u_{n}(s),P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y))}_{H}\bigr\}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s.

From the Taylor formula, it follows that there exists a positive constant
{c}_{p}>0
such that the following inequality holds

\displaystyle|\phi(x+h)-\phi(x)-\phi^{\prime}(x)h|

\displaystyle=\bigl||x+h{|}_{H}^{p}-|x{|}_{H}^{p}-p|x{|}_{H}^{p-2}{(x,h)}_{H}\bigr|

(G.3)

\displaystyle\leq{c}_{p}({|x|}_{H}^{p-2}+{|h|}_{H}^{p-2})\,|h{|}_{H}^{2},\;\;x,h\in H.

Let us define a (local) process
{M}_{n}
and a process
{I}_{n}
by respectively

\displaystyle{M}_{n}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{Y}}\big\{|u_{n}(s-)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s-),y){|}^{p}-|u_{n}(s-){|}^{p}\bigr\}\,\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y),

(G.4)

\displaystyle t\in[0,T\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)],

and

\displaystyle{\displaystyle I}_{n}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{Y}}\big\{|u_{n}(s)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y){|}^{p}-|u_{n}(s){|}^{p}

(G.5)

\displaystyle-p|u_{n}(s){|}^{p-2}{(u_{n}(s),P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y))}_{H}\bigr\}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s,\quad t\in[0,T].

By (
G.3
), (
3.7
) and (
G.1
), we deduce that the stopped process

\bigl({M}_{n}(t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)){\bigr)}_{t\in[0,T]}
,
is a square integrable martingale. Hence we infer that

\mathbb{E}[{M}_{n}(t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R))]=0\mbox{ for all }t\in[0,T].

(G.6)

Let us define another auxiliary process
By (
G.3
) and (
3.7
) we we infer that
for some constant
{\tilde{c}}_{p}>0
, the following inequalities hold,

\displaystyle|{I}_{n}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq{c}_{p}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Y}|P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y){|}_{H}^{2}\bigl\{|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p-2}+|P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y){|}_{H}^{p-2}\bigr\}\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq{c}_{p}\int_{0}^{t}\bigl\{{C}_{2}|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p-2}\bigl(1+|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{2}\bigr)+{C}_{p}\bigl(1+|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p}\bigr)\bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq{\tilde{c}}_{p}\int_{0}^{t}\bigl\{1+|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p}\bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}s={\tilde{c}}_{p}t+{\tilde{c}}_{p}\int_{0}^{t}|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p}\,\mathrm{d}s,\qquad t\in[0,T].

Thus by the Fubini Theorem, we obtain the following inequality

\mathbb{E}\bigl[|{I}_{n}(t)|\bigr]\leq{\tilde{c}}_{p}t+{\tilde{c}}_{p}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p}\bigr]\,\mathrm{d}s,\qquad t\in[0,T].

(G.7)

By (
G.2
) and (
G.7
), we have for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl|{u}_{n}({t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}){\bigr|}_{H}^{p}\bigr]+\frac{p}{2}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\int_{0}^{T\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p-2}{\|u_{n}(s)\}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr]

(G.8)

\displaystyle\leq c+{\tilde{c}}_{p}T+(c_{1}+{\tilde{c}}_{p})\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p}\bigr]\,\mathrm{d}s.

In particular, for
t\in[0,T]
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[|{u}_{n}({t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}){|}_{H}^{p}\bigr]\leq c+{\tilde{c}}_{p}T+(c_{1}+{\tilde{c}}_{p})\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|u_{n}(s\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)){|}_{H}^{p}\bigr]\,\mathrm{d}s.

By the Gronwall Lemma we infer that there exists a constant
{\tilde{\tilde{C_{p}}}}
independent of
R>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[|{u}_{n}({t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}){|}^{p}\bigr]\leq{\tilde{\tilde{C_{p}}}},\;\;t\in[0,T].

Therefore,

\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|{u}_{n}(t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)){|}_{H}^{p}\bigr]\leq{\tilde{\tilde{C_{p}}}}

and in particular, for some constant
\tilde{{C}_{p}}>0
,

\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\int_{0}^{{T\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}}|{u}_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p}\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr]\leq\tilde{{C}_{p}}.

Passing to the limit as
R\to\infty
, by the Fatou Lemma we infer that

\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\int_{0}^{T}|{u}_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p}\,ds\Bigr]\leq\tilde{{C}_{p}}.

(G.9)

By (
G.8
) and (
G.9
), we infer that, for some positive constant
{C}_{p}
,

\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\int_{0}^{T\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p-2}{\|u_{n}(s)\|}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr]\leq{C}_{p}.

(G.10)

Passing to the limit as
R\to\infty
and using again the Fatou Lemma we infer that

\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\int_{0}^{T}|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p-2}{\|u_{n}(s)\|}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr]\leq{C}_{p}.

(G.11)

In particular, putting
p:=2
by (
2.16
) (
G.11
) and (
G.9
) we obtain assertion (
4.2
).

Let us move to the proof of inequality (
4.3
).
From now we assume that
p:=4+\gamma
.
By the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{r\in[0,t]}|{M}_{n}(r\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R))|\Bigr]

(G.12)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\Bigl|\int_{0}^{r\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\int_{Y}\big\{\bigl|u_{n}({s}^{-}\!)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}({s}^{-});y){\bigr|}_{H}^{p}-\bigl|u_{n}({s}^{-}\!){\bigr|}_{H}^{p}\bigr\}\tilde{\eta}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y)\Bigr|\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq{\tilde{K}}_{2,1}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\int_{Y}\bigl(\bigl|u_{n}({s}\!)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}({s}),y){\bigr|}_{H}^{p}-\bigl|u_{n}({s}){\bigr|}_{H}^{p}{\bigr)}^{2}\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s{\Bigr)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr]

for some constant
{\tilde{K}}_{2,1}>0
.
Using the Mean Value Theorem we infer that there exists a constant
{\tilde{c}}_{p}>0
such that for all
x,h\in H
,

\displaystyle\bigl||x+h{|}_{H}^{p}-|x{|}_{H}^{p}{\bigr|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\;{\tilde{c}}_{p}({|x|}_{H}^{p-1}{|h|}_{H}+{|h|}_{H}^{p}{)}^{2}

(G.13)

\displaystyle=\;{\tilde{c}}_{p}({|x|}_{H}^{2p-2}|h{|}_{H}^{2}+2{|x|}_{H}^{p-1}{|h|}_{H}^{p+1}+{|h|}_{H}^{2p}).

Hence by inequality (
3.7
) we obtain for all
s\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\int_{Y}\bigl({u_{n}(s)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y)}_{H}^{p}-{u_{n}(s)}_{H}^{p}{\bigr)}^{2}\nu(\mathrm{d}y)

(G.14)

\displaystyle\leq{\tilde{c}}_{p}{u_{n}(s)}_{H}^{2p-2}\int_{Y}{F(s,u_{n}(s),y)}_{H}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle+{\tilde{c}}_{p}{u_{n}(s)}_{H}^{p-1}\int_{Y}{F(s,u_{n}(s),y)}_{H}^{p+1}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)+{\tilde{c}}_{p}\int_{Y}{F(s,u_{n}(s),y)}_{H}^{2p}\nu(dy)

\displaystyle\leq{\tilde{c}}_{p}\Bigl\{{C}_{2}{u_{n}(s)}_{H}^{2p-2}(1+{u_{n}(s)}_{H}^{2})+{C}_{5+\gamma}{u_{n}(s)}_{H}^{p-1}(1+{u_{n}(s)}_{H}^{p+1})+{C}_{2(4+\gamma)}(1+{u_{n}(s)}_{H}^{2p})\Bigr\}.

By (
G.14
) and the Young inequality we infer that

\int_{Y}\bigl(|u_{n}(s)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s);y){|}_{H}^{p}-|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p}{\bigr)}^{2}\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\leq{K}_{1}+{K}_{2}|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{2p}

for some positive constants
{K}_{1}
and
{K}_{2}
. Thus

\displaystyle\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}\int_{Y}\bigl(|u_{n}(s)+P_{n}F(s,u_{n}(s),y){|}_{H}^{p}-|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p}{\bigr)}^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}s{\Bigr)}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{T{K}_{1}}+\sqrt{{K}_{2}}\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{2p}\,\mathrm{d}s{\Bigr)}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(G.15)

By (
G.12
), (
G.15
) and (
G.9
)
we obtain the following inequalities

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{r\in[0,t]}|{M}_{n}(r\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R))|\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq{\tilde{K}}_{p}\sqrt{T{K}_{1}}+{\tilde{K}}_{p}\sqrt{{K}_{2}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{2p}\,\mathrm{d}s{\Bigr)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq{\tilde{K}}_{p}\sqrt{T{K}_{1}}+{\tilde{K}}_{p}\sqrt{{K}_{2}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|u_{n}({s\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}){|}_{H}^{p}{\Bigr)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p}\,\mathrm{d}s{\Bigr)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq{\tilde{K}}_{p}\sqrt{T{K}_{1}}+\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|u_{n}(s\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)){|}_{H}^{p}\Bigr]+{\tilde{K}}_{p}^{2}{K}_{2}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\int_{0}^{t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}|u_{n}(s){|}_{H}^{p}\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|u_{n}({s\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}){|}_{H}^{p}\Bigr]+\tilde{K},

(G.16)

where
\tilde{K}={\tilde{K}}_{p}\sqrt{T{K}_{1}}+{\tilde{K}}_{p}^{2}{K}_{2}{\tilde{C}}_{p}
, with the constant
{\tilde{C}}_{p}
is the same as in (
G.9
).
Therefore, by (
G.2
) ,

\displaystyle|{u}_{n}({t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)}){|}^{p}\leq c+{c}_{1}\int_{0}^{T}|u_{n}(s){|}^{p}\,\mathrm{d}s+\sup_{r\in[0,T]}|{M}_{n}(r\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R))|

\displaystyle+|{I}_{n}(T\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R))|,\mbox{ for all $t\in[0,T]$},

(G.17)

where
{I}_{n}
is defined by (
G.5
). Since inequality (
G.17
) holds for all
t\in[0,T]
and the right-hand side of (
G.17
) in independent of
t
, we infer that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|{u}_{n}(t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)){|}^{p}\Bigr]\leq c+{c}_{1}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\int_{0}^{T}|u_{n}(s){|}^{p}\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{r\in[0,T]}|{M}_{n}(r\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R))|\Bigr]+\mathbb{E}\Bigl[|{I}_{n}(T\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R))|\Bigr].

(G.18)

Using inequalities (
G.9
), (
G.16
) and (
G.7
) in (
G.18
) we infer that

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{t\in T}|{u}_{n}(t\wedge{\tau}_{n}(R)){|}^{p}\Bigr]\leq{C}_{1}

for some constant
{C}_{1}
independent of
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
R>0
. Passing to the limit as
R\to\infty
, we obtain inequality (
4.3
) for
p=4+\gamma
.
To obtain inequality (
4.3
) for
p\in[1,4+\gamma)
it suffices to apply the Jensen inequality.
The proof of Lemma
4.2
is thus complete. ∎

Appendix H
